**🆕 2026-08-10 — Nvidia takes a stake in the power, not just the silicon.** NVDA has agreed to invest **$2bn into Lancium** — the power-infrastructure developer behind the **OpenAI/Oracle Stargate campus in Texas** — for **~20%**, and to commit **another $1bn as Lancium secures additional planned power**, which would take it to **~30%** once campuses hit thresholds including grid hookups. The deal values Lancium and its land/power-connection portfolio at **~$10bn enterprise value** including the investment and debt. Backer: **Blackstone**. Stated purpose: **secure multiple gigawatts of pending power rights for Nvidia's chip customers' data projects** as energy becomes the binding constraint (The Information / Ann Davis Vaughan, Valida Pau, Phoebe Liu, 2026-08-07-08; DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10). Vital Knowledge frames it inside the broader capital-structure point — **"the AI industry is demanding capital far above what companies are generating via operating cash flow"** — and notes SemiAnalysis speculation that **Nvidia could provide enormous vendor financing to SpaceX** for Musk's compute build-out (Vital Knowledge / Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-10). Cross-ref [SPCX.md](SPCX.md), [OPENAI.md](OPENAI.md), [ORCL.md](ORCL.md).

**🆕 The VR300 de-spec is now sized.** UBS checks: first VR300 variant **384GB HBM4/GPU** (8-Hi × 24Gb × 8 × 2 dies), a later version **512GB HBM4E/GPU** around **C3Q27** — *below* UBS's prior 768GB assumption — with the de-spec driven by **persistent HBM supply tightness**; the offset is **more VR300 units in the chain** (UBS / Tim Arcuri, 2026-08-10). Downstream confirmation of the timing slip: **KYEC guided Q3 sales to grow only high-single-digits with GM 37-38% (down q/q) on an Nvidia push-out**, cutting its GM guide to 36-37% from 40%+, and implying **4Q26 revenue +15%+** as **Nvidia and Broadcom ramp in 4Q26, extending into 1Q27** (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-10). Cross-ref [MU.md](MU.md), [AVGO.md](AVGO.md).

**Q1 FY27 (2026-05-20):** Revenue $82B, +85% y/y / +20% q/q — 3rd straight quarter of y/y acceleration, 14th straight q/q gain. Data Center $75B (+92% y/y); compute $60B, networking $15B (nearly tripled y/y). Hyperscale $38B (+12% q/q); ACIE $37B (+31% q/q, AI cloud >3x y/y); sovereign +80% y/y. Non-GAAP GM 75% (flat). Record FCF $49B (vs $35B in Q4). Returned record $20B to holders; dividend raised $0.01→$0.25, new $80B buyback on top of $39B remaining; ~50% of FCF to be returned this year.
- **Guide:** Q2 FY27 revenue **$91B ±2%**, growth led by Data Center. GM mid-70s for FY. **No China DC compute in the outlook** (H200 licenses approved but no revenue recognized).
- **Roadmap:** Vera Rubin production shipments begin **2H, starting Q3 (Oct qtr)**, ramping into Q4/Q1; mgmt expects every cloud/model builder to deploy it. Reiterated **$1T Blackwell+Rubin revenue visibility cal-25 through cal-27**. New **Vera standalone CPU = $200B TAM**, with ~$20B CPU revenue visibility this year (Vivek Arya Q&A: the $20B is standalone). Jensen frames AI infra spend reaching **$3–4T/yr by end of decade**; hyperscale capex >$1T in 2027.
- **Management's own TAM framing — GTC FY26 analyst deck (NVDA GTC Financial Analyst Q&A, 2025-03-19):** the deck lays out the three TAM pillars NVDA now sells against: a **$1T computing inflection point** (accelerated-computing transition), **AI infrastructure for ~$500B enterprise IT** (new compute + networking + storage + software), and **physical AI / robotics framed as a ~$50T industrial-and-robotics** opportunity — the largest, longest-dated leg of the story and the strategic rationale behind the Automotive/physical-AI and Omniverse push. On product, the deck positions **Vera Rubin + NVIDIA Photonics as the "annual rhythm for the world to build out AI infrastructure"** (i.e. management formalizing the one-architecture-per-year cadence), with **Blackwell in full production = "fastest ramp in company history."** On inference economics it claims **Blackwell = 40X Hopper**, and **Blackwell NVL72 + Dynamo = 40X for inference with reasoning models 100X one-shot** — the management-stated efficiency case underpinning the tokens-per-watt / AI-factory-ROI bull argument. Read-through: these are aspirational/strategic TAMs (not bottoms-up models) but anchor how Jensen sizes the runway — the $50T physical-AI figure dwarfs the data-center TAM and is the part the sell-side has yet to underwrite.
- **Rubin power step-up (JPM AI Datacenters & Power, 2025-11-04):** GPU TDP growth accelerating to **50%+** in Rubin vs. the 20-30% historical CAGR; **Rubin Ultra = 4 dies/chip (~5.3kW) vs. Rubin's 2 dies (~2.3kW)**. Rack design power scales DGX B200/B300 ~20kW → **GB200 NVL72 ~198kW → GB300 NVL72 ~288kW → VR200 ~330-444kW → VR Ultra ~1.5MW**, with the 800V HVDC power rack arriving on VR Ultra. Blackwell/Rubin GPUs now ~70-72% of rack system power (vs. 36%/47% for A100/H100). Critically, JPM models NVDA high-end GPU **units declining ~25% in 2028** (6,655k → 4,991k) purely on die-count rising (2→4 dies/chip) — unit prints will understate revenue/compute. A new high-end **CPX** SKU adds TAM from 2027 (assumes 144 CPX/rack, 10-15% attach 2027/28). JPM keeps 2025/26 NVDA GPU shipment forecasts ~unchanged but lifts the GB/VR server mix in 2026-28.
- **Scale-up / networking (MS Scale-up primer):** NVLink delivers ~7.2 Tbit/s per GPU today, doubling to **14.4 Tbit/s per GPU in Rubin** via bi-directional SerDes; in NVL72 (GB200/300) all 72 GPUs are clustered exclusively over NVLink, and **NVLink Fusion** extends it to hybrid custom-ASIC/CPU configs (MS reads this as an NVLink extension, not a true open play). Outside NVDA's stack the scale-up greenfield (UALink, Broadcom's Scale-up Ethernet, PCIe) is where ALAB/AVGO/MRVL compete — i.e. the merchant attack is on the non-NVDA accelerators, not NVLink itself.
- Q4 FY26 (2026-02-25) context: rev $68B (+73%), FY26 DC $194B (+68%), FY26 FCF $97B; ~9 GW Blackwell deployed; SBC now in non-GAAP from Q1 FY27.
- **Vera Rubin timing — Q4, not Q3 (BofA Vivek Arya, NVDA setup call, 2026-05-19):** Arya's plan-of-record read is Rubin ships *to the supply chain* in Q3 (revenue recognized as it shipped Blackwell ahead of end-customer receipt — cf. the ~$11B Blackwell shipped-but-not-received quarter), but the **real big external-customer ramp is late this year into early next year**, i.e. a **Q4 launch with real numbers** (~$0 plan for Q3 public instances). Rubin uses extreme engineering on HBM4 nobody else is running yet; NVDA had ~2-3 designs ready and at least one works well even on HBM4 (10-11 Gbit/s specs). The Hopper→Blackwell jump (8→72 GPU scale-up domain) was far harder; Rubin stays in the NVL72 scale-up domain so the ramp should be cleaner.
- **Rack-power / 800V HVDC arrives on Rubin Ultra, NOT Vera Rubin (AlphaSense 800V expert call, 2026-06-03):** Two ex-Google/Eaton DC-infra experts frame **NVIDIA's Kyber rack (2027/2028) as *the* inflection point for 800V DC** in the data center; Vera Rubin (mid-2027) still ships on a **480V (US) / 415V (EU) power shelf** per NVDA's own documentation. NVDA told them last year the 800V move targets **~7% electricity-efficiency gain** (lab studies: ~78% AC-system efficiency → ~94% with 800V DC) — explicitly so the customer "can take that 7% and buy more chips." Adoption is component-gated (power shelves, DC breakers, solid-state transformers) and safety-gated (arc-flash at 800V/10kA); sidecar power likely bridges first, with an OCP-driven UL spec due before year-end. One large enterprise/hyperscaler-crossover buyer has stated it won't adopt DC until 2032 — i.e. the transition is "evolution, not revolution." Read-through: confirms 800V HVDC content steps up on VR Ultra/Kyber (consistent with JPM's roadmap), and that the marginal rack-power $ accrues 2027+.
- **CPU "$20B" disclosure — back-solving the units (Citi/Jefferies Asia recal, 2026-05-22):** NVDA's standalone-CPU $20B framing implies an implausible unit count at a ~$5-6k ASP (~7-8M units) or AMD-like ASP (~10M units) — the analyst's reconciliation is the number bundles NVLink + the head-node CPU (~3-4M head nodes) + Smart-NIC CPUs + true standalone, not a clean merchant-CPU print. Arm royalty leverage is mostly "companion CPUs" (fixed CPU:GPU ratio in AI servers, ~99% Arm in GB/VR), with the agentic-CPU TAM still largely x86-native software. Read-through: corroborates Arcuri's ~$5B "apples-to-apples chip" haircut on the $20B Vera number; **Google Axion mix at ~60% and AWS Graviton ~60% of general-purpose server mix** were higher than expected (x86 volume headwind).
- **HBM-price / BOM-consignment risk (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23):** NVIDIA HBM pricing goes from **$17-18/GB (2026) → $30-32/GB (2027), a +70-80% step** (pricing for others likely even higher). At that level the **GPU chip + HBM alone reaches ~$4.3M** per rack (~60% of BOM at a 75% NVDA HBM margin; ~$20→ implied chip $55-60K ×72), and the **total memory bill (incl. SOCAMM/LPDDR5/NAND) approaches ~$200B next year**. The live risk: if hyperscalers refuse to let NVDA keep a ~75% margin on a ~40-60% BOM slice, they could push to **consign HBM directly**, which compresses NVDA revenue, ASP and EPS — "the stock that looks cheap isn't so cheap anymore." On NAND, **Kioxia's Super IO (XL Flash, SLC, ultra-low-latency for agentic workloads) has NVIDIA as a partner** (3M units '25 → 10M '26 → 100M '27 target) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23).
- **NVDA gets the LOWEST HBM price; LPDDR5 the 2027 pinch (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** because NVIDIA's HBM volume is the largest and the buyer pool is small, **NVIDIA is expected to receive the lowest HBM price** of any customer (AMD, Broadcom, AWS all priced higher); HBM4 must roughly triple to ~$5/Gb in 2027 (from ~$2.05/Gb) to keep suppliers allocating HBM wafers. Separately, **Low Power DDR5 becomes the single tightest-supply product in 2027** as NVIDIA's Vera / next-gen CPU (SOCAMM) and smartphones compete for it — potential for QoQ price acceleration to resume (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23).
- **HBM roadmap / "most profitable HBM vendor" framing (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2025-08-11):** NVDA commands the "lion's share" of HBM demand through 2027 despite the ASIC rise; **Rubin Ultra pushes per-GPU HBM capacity to 1TB** (roadmap: A100's 80GB HBM2E → 1024GB HBM4E on Rubin Ultra; H100 80GB@3TB/s → GB200 192GB@8TB/s). NVDA (and AMD) are expected to ship custom HBM4 base-die implementations first (2025/26), with other ASIC designers not following until HBM4E (~2027) — a structural lead time advantage baked into the memory supply chain. The GH100 die alone carries 12 HBM controllers on-die; Rubin/MI400-class designs add off-package LPDDR as a second memory tier via the base-die PHY, and Rubin Ultra's oblong single-row-of-SOCs package (vs Blackwell's dual-SOC MCM square grid) doubles usable HBM sites vs Blackwell's 8-site max. The report's framing: "arguably it is Nvidia that is the most profitable HBM vendor in the world" — it captures gross margin on HBM shipped inside its own accelerators, which corroborates (and predates, by ~10 months) the wiki's own 2026-06 Redburn/StoneX HBM-margin-capture debate below. Samsung's HBM3E was qualified specifically for NVDA's H20; NVDA was "well supplied" with HBM3E in 2025, with demand shifting to HBM4 as Rubin ramps (SemiAnalysis, 2025-08-11).
- **Memory shortage forcing DRAM "nerfing" (MS Joe Moore, memory+semicap call, 2026-06-22):** MS flags **NVDA cutting LPDDR5 content roughly in half in the Vera Rubin racks** — read not as bad news but as a tell of how acute the memory shortage is ("everything is being nerfed"); when supply loosens "NVDA's going to double the memory in their racks again." Moore reads **DRAM as the primary bottleneck for AI builds right now, worse than the CPU shortage**, with HBM/most server DRAM for next year already being told to customers as sold out (JPM TMC, 2026-05-22 corroborates). Fast-token economics also a demand tailwind: a 2x-faster token costs ~10-12x more, so low-latency silicon (Groq/Cerebras, which NVDA commercialized via the Groq LPU) is a niche ~10-20% of the market, not a GPU substitute.
- **Per-GW economics / capex pull-through (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16):** JPM models NVIDIA DC capex at **$45-55B per GW** (vs $35-40B AMD, $30-40B Broadcom ASIC), of which **NVDA compute+networking revenue is $25-35B per GW** ($20-28B compute, $5-7B networking) — roughly 2x AMD's $15-20B/GW capture. NVDA GPU unit shipments modeled **6.2M (2025) → 8.9M (2026E, +39%) → 9.9M (2027E)**; total accelerator units (GPU+ASIC) 10.1M→16.3M→23.3M, with GPUs falling from 68% of units (2025) to 58% (2026E) to 47% (2027E) as ASICs out-ship GPUs (ASIC units +109% y/y in 2026 vs GPU +39%). OpenAI's NVDA deal (≥10 GW, ≥$100B NVDA equity in OAI disbursed per-GW) + first Vera Rubin deployments in 2H26; aggregate OpenAI compute commitments (NVDA+AMD+AVGO) >$1T over 4-5 yrs with 50-60% to compute partners.
- **Monetization-per-GW inflecting up (BG2 podcast, 2026-06):** AI monetization rate per GW has risen from ~$20-25B (early 2026 best case) to ~$30-40B+; recent signed deals — xAI/Anthropic ~$22-23B/GW, xAI/Google ~$50B/GW — vs an implied ~$14B/GW on SpaceX's leaked $160B 2028 revenue. Panel frames this as direct support for NVDA demand: in a watt-constrained world, NVDA's superior tokens-per-watt = more revenue per GW even where alt-silicon is cheaper. NVDA has "maintained share very handsomely" the last few years and likely *gained* share ex-Anthropic in 2025-26; panel skeptical AMD lands the full ~6 of 27 GW (~30% of openly-committed GW) it's on paper for. Gavin Baker: ">200B inference revenue exiting 2026," MS 2027 hyperscale capex chart lifted $950B→$1.1T (panel thinks ~$1.5T incl. SpaceX/neoclouds).
- **Hardware perf vs alt-silicon (SemiAnalysis "DeepSeek V4 Day 0-43", 2026-06-09):** On DeepSeek V4 1.6T inference, **GB300 NVL72 dominates all systems** — cost per Mtok **$0.156** at 50 tok/s/user (8k in/1k out) with MTP — its NVL72 72-GPU single NVLink domain keeps the MoE all-to-all on NVLink (vs B200/B300 8-GPU NVLink islands hitting the scale-out wall earlier). CUDA + native SGLang/vLLM had working Day-0 support (only other Day-0 stack was Huawei CANN); NVDA's own TensorRT-LLM had bugs and lagged. B200 tokens/all-in-MW rose ~300k→~500k (+1.7x) on pure software gains (fixed 2.17 kW/GPU). AMD MI355X was a "Day-0 disappointment" (FP8-only, 1-2 tok/s/user) but +100x in <1 month, eventually edging H200 at low interactivity — still sits behind on scale-up domain size and collective-stack maturity.
- **Hyperscaler-vs-neocloud segment reporting, one quarter in (Barron's/Adam Levine, "Nvidia Needs a New Narrative, and Jensen Huang Has a Plan", 2026-05-25):** the new Data Center split shows **hyperscalers (Amazon, Microsoft, Alphabet, Meta, Oracle) at 47% of DC sales in fiscal 2025 → 55% on a trailing-12-month basis**, vs "AI Clouds, Industrial & Enterprise" (neoclouds like CoreWeave, corporates, governments) the balance. Q1 hyperscaler revenue **+115% y/y**; smaller-customer revenue **+74% y/y**. Networking chip sales **+142% y/y to $31bn** — Nvidia now the biggest data-center networking company outright. Huang, on the call, flagged the ACIE bucket as under-covered: "This second category is fairly poorly understood because there are just so many companies, and each one of the installations [is] relatively small compared to, of course, one of the hyperscalers." Despite the beat, **the stock fell ~3% over the two days after the print** — Barron's frames Huang's own line, "we should be growing faster than hyperscale capex," as "a key point of concern for investors" given hyperscaler growth (+115%) is now outpacing the smaller-customer bucket (+74%) (Barron's/Adam Levine, 2026-05-25).
- **FY2026 profit framing (Barron's "Top CEOs of 2026" — Jensen Huang profile, Jack Hough, 2026-06-22):** consensus estimate for Nvidia profit this year is **$217 billion**, which would shatter the current single-year corporate-profit record; "analysts see Nvidia doubling that figure in the following three years" (Barron's/Jack Hough, 2026-06-22).
- **Vera CPU / Rubin GPU units raised (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01):** UBS raises its **Vera CPU units estimate to 5.5mn in 2027** (from **1.6mn in 2026**), implying **57% growth in NVDA's CoWoS demand in 2027**. Rubin GPU ramp was slower in Q2'26 on redesigns but UBS still expects Rubin to reach **2.1mn units for 2026** with a steep 2H'26 pickup (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01). Cross-check: this 2.1mn 2026 Rubin figure sits just above Citi/Jefferies' "~2M units in Q4" (2026-05-22) and Nomura's "~2M Rubin units in 2026F, cut from a prior 2.5M" (2026-06-30) — broadly consistent across brokers.
- **GPU rental pricing + LLM token demand (JPM Data Center Watch — Cardoso/Singh/Vitenzon/Chauhan, 2026-07-01):** GPU rental prices rose across all NVDA tiers (A100, H100, B200) in June m/m, led by A100; B200 pricing ~2x H100, H100 ~1.7x A100, both ratios narrowing slightly for a 2nd straight month. Separately, OpenRouter LLM token volume **+70% m/m (20x y/y)** in June; token pricing +7% m/m but still -5% y/y; token spending **+70% m/m (16x y/y)**. Anthropic's Claude Opus 4.7 was the only model ranking top-5 in both volume and spend on OpenRouter in June. US-based models were 35% of OpenRouter volume (down from 46% May, 56% April) but 85%+ of spend (JPM Data Center Watch, 2026-07-01).
- **SemiAnalysis accelerator model, cited via Jefferies (Jeffrey Favuzza, 2026-06-30):** estimates NVDA datacenter compute revenue **~20% higher than consensus for 2H FY27**; Rubin ramp expected to be large after being delayed by earlier HBM4 issues (now resolved) and front-end wafer supply now built up (Jefferies/Favuzza citing SemiAnalysis, 2026-06-30).
- **Semi inventory tracker (Morgan Stanley · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-01):** MS prefers NVDA among compute/networking names given tightening supply/demand and defensible inventory risk; overall semi supply chain inventory still **33 days above historical median**, no broad restocking cycle yet (MS Semiconductor Inventory Tracker/Joseph Moore, 2026-07-01).
- **xAI Colossus 1 resold to Anthropic (JPM, via META extraction, 2026-06-30):** JPM reports xAI sold its Colossus 1 compute capacity to Anthropic for **$1.25bn/month**, containing **220,000+ NVIDIA GPUs** (H100, H200, GB200). Read-through: next-gen GPU/compute remains in shortage even as older-gen (H100/A100) capacity gets resold (JPM/Wei Zheng Chan desk, 2026-06-30).
- **Kyber rack delayed 12+ months to 2028 — scale-up roadmap gap for Rubin Ultra (SemiAnalysis, via CNBC & MS TMT, 2026-07-06):** SemiAnalysis reports NVDA has delayed the next-gen **Kyber NVL144 rack by 12+ months to 2028** due to **PCB midplane manufacturability** issues. **NVL576** (8× Oberon racks connected over CPO between NVSwitches) is also likely **delayed or restricted to small volumes** given current CPO challenges, and the proposed **NVL72x2 back-to-back** alternative (two Oberon racks back-to-back to grow the copper NVLink scale-up world size) has been **cancelled** after heavy CSP/hyperscaler pushback (odd design, operational burden). Because **CPO NVSwitch won't be available until Feynman**, NVDA currently has **no proven solution to expand the scale-up world size for Rubin Ultra** — leaving an opening for **AMD MI500X** and **GOOGL TPUv8i 'Broadfly'** to gain scale-up advantages. This is a genuine competitive-risk nuance (bear-ish for the scale-up roadmap), sharpening the earlier Jefferies (06-22) "Kyber slips to ≥2028, Rubin Ultra stays on Oberon/NVL72" read with the specific manufacturability cause and the NVL576/NVL72x2 fallout. (SemiAnalysis, via CNBC & MS TMT · 2026-07-06)
- **NVDA as the AI-debt "central bank" (SemiAnalysis, via Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-06):** SemiAnalysis frames the AI debt-financing market as set to **exceed $7T by 2029** — which would be the 2nd-largest ABS sector after mortgages — with NVDA acting as the "central bank" of this financing by providing **revenue guarantees to neoclouds** so that data centers without a hyperscaler anchor customer can still be built. Extends the 07-02 The-Information "NVDA backstops customers' GPUs for a cut of cloud revenue" datapoint (below) into a systemic-scale framing. Bull: de-risks the buildout and locks in the ecosystem; bear: another sign the cycle increasingly depends on NVDA-provided financial engineering (circular-financing critique). (SemiAnalysis, via Vital Knowledge · 2026-07-06)
- **GPU backstop program — mechanics + read from the primary note (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity", 2026-07-06):** the full paid note quantifies the backstop the 07-06 Vital-Knowledge/07-02 The-Information items summarized. In the program NVDA gives Neoclouds a **take-or-pay commitment — a minimum-revenue guarantee on the underlying GPU capacity** — and in exchange **shares in a portion of the Neocloud's revenue earned above the backstop level**; the Neocloud stays free to rent to any customer at any tenor, and **the intent is the backstop is never actually invoked** (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop...", 2026-07-06). Objectives: (1) broaden compute access beyond a few hyperscalers/large AI labs and enable **shorter-than-5yr rental tenors**, (2) buy lenders time up the risk-pricing learning curve, (3) grow Neoclouds into bankable, diversified-client-book platforms that can also press custom-silicon against NVDA's systems. Because lenders lean on NVDA's **AA/Aa2 investment-grade** balance sheet behind the backstop, it unlocks all three legs of the **"AI Project Trinity" (Capital, Offtake, Datacenter)** for Neoclouds that otherwise can't raise debt outside a 5yr IG-hyperscaler-backstopped template. NVDA is also **directly leasing datacenter capacity and subleasing to Neoclouds** to close the hardest (Datacenter) leg. Crucially for the demand debate, SemiAnalysis reiterates the market is **still materially below its supply-chain-tracked Accelerator Model on NVDA's 2H 2026 GPU shipment volumes and revenue** — 3 years into the buildout. Macro backdrop from the same note: **>$7T AI debt outstanding by 2029** (2nd-largest ABS market after US MBS ~$13T), annual AI capex **>$2T in 2028**, and cumulative AI capex 2024–2029 **~$11.1T**, with Oracle→Meta→Google increasingly funding buildouts with debt. Bull: NVDA grows the pie and takes a bigger, recurring, near-pure-margin slice; bear: sharpens the circular-financing critique. (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop...", 2026-07-06)
- **Backstop economics — the modelled numbers (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity: Capital, Offtake and Datacenters", 2026-07-06; net-new quantification added on the 2026-07-24 dedup pass off the primary SemiAnalysis email copy):** the arithmetic behind the mechanics bullet above, none of which was on the page. **Term is typically six years**, over which Nvidia stands ready to purchase compute at pre-agreed price levels that vary through the period; above the floor Nvidia takes a **revenue share modelled at 40–60%, working out to a ~18–20% take rate on average over the life of the deal**. The illustrative curve SemiAnalysis presents (deliberately at the *low end* of the backstop range) averages **$2.36/hr over the six years, starting at $3.68/hr in year 1** against an assumed $6.75/hr year-1 customer rental price. **Accounting — the part that matters for the balance-sheet debate:** these deals sit on a **cloud service agreement line, "a contingent guarantee rather than a funded asset — making this an off-balance-sheet line unless triggered"**; the revenue share itself is **near-pure margin and recurring**. **Modelled trajectory:** cloud service agreements reach **$77.5B by end-F1/27 and up to $175.3B by end-F1/29**, i.e. **~$5.9B of contingent guarantee added per 100MW backstopped**, driving **incremental NVDA revenue of $1.8B in F1/27 rising to $13.9B in F1/29**, on **932MW under the program in F1/27 (of which 432MW already announced — 360MW Firmus + 72MW SharonAI), plus 1,000MW in F1/28 and 1,500MW in F1/29**. **Calibration (why lenders bite and operators don't get lazy):** a neocloud earns **25.4% IRR on a 1-year rental book under the backstop vs 40.7% unbackstopped**, and **~zero or slightly negative if the backstop is actually triggered** — sized so banks clear a **DSCR of at least 1.3x in the backstop-activated scenario, typically a 70–80% LTV**. Strategically the note frames Nvidia's buyers as **four concentric pools** (all buyers → neoclouds → certified NCPs → NCPs with a backstop), each narrower and each worth more to Nvidia: at the innermost pool "the one-time hardware sale becomes a recurring, near-pure-margin incremental revenue stream, and Nvidia gains a partial claim on the downstream rental economics it used to give away at the point of sale" — the "**Nvidia as the Central Bank of AI**" framing already summarized on this page, now with the mechanism attached. **Announced deals (all APAC so far):** **SharonAI** — 72MW AI factory in Australia, scaling to as many as **40,000 GB300s** under a six-year backstop, **$4.88B disclosed backstop value = ~$2.33/hr/GPU implied floor**, footprint growing to 132MW with 102MW contracted and >55,000 Nvidia GPUs by mid-2027; **Firmus** — **360MW in Batam, Indonesia** (announced 2026-06-29, likely a DayOne facility), guiding **$25–30B of customer revenue over six years**. Scale check on how new this all is: Nvidia's program **started at an $860M lease guarantee in October 2025 (in exchange for warrants) and had risen to $3.5B by January 2026** (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24) — versus the $77.5B/$175.3B trajectory modelled above. **The bear side SemiAnalysis itself keeps:** three named obstacles to the market ever reaching ~$7.1T — (1) "**Hyperscaler balance sheets will not be able to backstop trillions of dollars' worth of compute**," and outside a 5-yr hyperscale-backstopped template "the appetite to lend drops off almost entirely"; (2) **lenders are still on the learning curve** — most banks have "a nascent understanding of AI Cluster total cost of ownership… and still hide behind the shield of an investment grade offtake or backstop"; (3) **no pricing/risk tooling** — GPU rental transactions are bilateral and unpublished and "there is no active derivatives market to provide a pricing signal or a good reference for GPU residual value." Also from the same note: **neocloud colocation leases carry a 3–5% higher yield-on-cost** than hyperscale/IG deals, and inference providers refuse tenors beyond 1yr, with some neoclouds extracting **prepays up to 100% of contract value** (a theoretically infinite IRR). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_ENC_Nvidia_GPU_Debt_Backstop_Unleashes_the_AI_Project_Trinity_Capital_Offtake_and_Datacenters_email.html)
- **Rubin vs MI455X — the microarchitecture and co-design case, quantified (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24):** SemiAnalysis's AMD Advancing-AI teardown is, read from the NVDA side, the most specific competitive scorecard on the page. **Compute:** MI455X marks **20 PF dense FP8 vs Rubin's 17,500 TFLOPs (17.5 PF)** — a mild AMD lead, but on far more silicon (3,470 mm² of logic per package, N2 vs everyone else's N3) and **"down to AMD's deficit to Nvidia in GPU microarchitecture design"**: MI455X **lacks the 3-bit LUT tensor cores Rubin's SM107 has**, which reduce the relative HBM-bandwidth requirement, and CDNA 5 "barely scales MMA shapes" where Nvidia has scaled them aggressively every generation. **Memory — a deliberate Nvidia gambit:** MI455X carries 12 HBM4 stacks / 432GB / **23.3 TB/s** vs Rubin's 8 stacks / 288GB / **22 TB/s**; Nvidia closed a 50%-wider-bus deficit by **raising targeted HBM4 pin speed to 10.7 Gbps — well above the original JEDEC HBM4 spec and 40% faster than AMD's 7.6 Gbps** — "precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X." Cost of that gambit: **memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4**, it **pushed out upstream Rubin output (now resolved)**, and **Nvidia "will be forced to use a much higher quality bin than AMD"** — the specific mechanism behind the HBM4-pricing/GM thread already on this page. Verdict: **"despite the delays, Vera Rubin will still be the first to deliver tokens at scale compared to MI455 Helios."** **Rack co-design — the clearest NVDA moat datapoint:** AMD's Helios is on a **slow rack production ramp specifically "due to the lack of a cableless design compared to Rubin Oberon rack"**; on scale-up, each Broadcom Tomahawk-6 switch in Helios uses only **432 of its 512 200G lanes** (over-provisioning forced by using a merchant switch), whereas "**Nvidia designed the 28.8T NVSwitch to match the needs of a 72 GPU rack**" and it divides evenly with no waste. **Power is the outlier in NVDA's favour:** MI455X draws **~240kW server / ~257kW all-in per rack vs GB300 NVL72's ~142kW / ~159kW** — and while MI455X screens competitive on TCO per *marketed* dense-FP8 PFLOP, SemiAnalysis flags AMD "has historically overstated TFLOPs" and suspects low MFU, so effective training throughput is likely lower. **The harsher note for NVDA, kept as written:** AMD gaining share "doesn't mean NVIDIA will do poorly — the pie is getting massive," but **"Jensen will need to cut bureaucracy & layers of different required internal stakeholder approvals, for even the simplest of tasks, at NVIDIA to move faster and defend their lead."** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Meta's whole GB200 fleet was the off-standard Ariel SKU; GB300 goes back to standard — a demand-mix positive (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21):** Meta's custom **Ariel NVL36x2** depopulated one GPU on the standard Bianca board to run **1 B200 per Grace** (vs the standard 2:1) for RecSys embeddings, at **14% higher TCO than a standard GB200 NVL72** and worse $/FLOP and $/HBM — "this decision cost Meta billions of dollars." **Meta's entire GB200 fleet was Ariel.** The 36x2 configuration was **dropped industry-wide for GB300 and Meta is now buying the standard configuration** — i.e. a large customer normalizing back onto NVDA's reference rack. Same note: Meta's software stack still depends on **Nvidia's version of RoCE**, so Meta was never realistically switching NIC vendors, and **GB300 deployments run Nvidia NICs** (and NSF) — a check on the merchant-networking-displacement bear. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **Design-cost / EDA lock-in as a cost-structure datapoint (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21):** NVIDIA's datacenter-GPU programs run **1,000+ engineers and $1B+ per major architecture**, including a **$100M+ Blackwell verification program**; NVIDIA's EDA-spend lock-in intensity (R²) rose **0.91 (2019) → 0.94 (2024)**, the highest in the fabless set. Context for the R&D line under the annual-cadence roadmap, not a thesis change. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **GPU shortage persists; subletting a net positive (MS Semis Weekly · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-06):** GPU shortage remains industry-wide; the subletting trend (e.g. META's excess-compute cloud, xAI's Colossus 1 resale) is a **net positive for NVDA share** — read as "excess demand, not excess supply." (MS Semis Weekly · Joseph Moore, 2026-07-06)
- **Per-GW customer-sales quantification + TPU-vs-GPU framing (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", Sunny Lin, 2026-07-02):** in its per-GW AI-server demand work UBS pegs NVDA's **customer sales per 1GW (rack only) at ~US$23.9bn (Blackwell) / ~US$24.4bn (Blackwell Ultra) / ~US$21.9bn (Rubin) / ~US$34.5bn (Rubin Ultra) / ~US$26.9bn (Feynman)** — vs ~US$17.5bn (AMD MI450) and ~US$17.4bn (Google TPU v7 Ironwood), i.e. NVDA racks carry the highest per-GW customer sales except at the Rubin/Feynman rack-count crossover. On foundry, NVDA's GPUs consume **~2-4kwpm of TSMC capacity per 1GW** (vs ~5kwpm for Broadcom's TPU v7p, ~1kwpm for AMD MI450) and NVDA holds **~57%/50%/47% of industry CoWoS capacity in 2025/26/27E**. UBS models NVDA **GPU sales ~US$318bn (2026E) → ~US$456bn (2027E)** and standalone **Vera CPU ~722k units (2026E) → ~2.03m (2027E)**. The note also frames Google's TPU (Ironwood) as having a **lower inferencing cost per token than Blackwell** on tokens-per-watt — the incumbent-GPU-vs-ASIC economics comparison underpinning the sector's share-fragmentation debate, against which NVDA is the benchmark. (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02)
- **Feynman as the root of the 800V/analog buildout (BofA "Watts to Tokens", Vivek Arya, 2026-05-25, read-through):** BofA's power-semis note makes NVDA's roadmap the explicit demand driver behind the whole grid-to-rack power revamp: rising compute density drives **rack power capacity up ~100x, from 10-15kW in traditional cloud servers to ~1.5MW for Nvidia's Feynman platform (CY29/30)** — GPU TDP (Hopper→Blackwell→Rubin→Rubin Ultra→Feynman) is the single biggest driver, so **NVDA's GPU/XPU roadmap is the reason the 800V DC transition + analog-semi content pool exists**. Sizing: **~233GW deployed through CY30** (of which NVDA ~105GW, ~51% — see [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md)), expanding the AI analog-semi market $7.9bn → $27bn (28% CAGR) inside a $1.7tn AI-DC-systems TAM. This is the distinct source-of-record behind the ~1.5MW Feynman figure the wiki also carries via JPM (Rubin Ultra ~1.5MW / 800V HVDC) — corroborated from BofA's semis desk. NVDA GPUs are also the compute substrate in JPM's SpaceX AI-DC power model (COLOSSUS ~325k NVIDIA GPUs; JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07 — see [SPCX.md](SPCX.md)). See [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_Watt_to_tokens.html)
- **Company NDR deck — roadmap cadence + pricing-durability + inference-economics evidence (NVIDIA "NDR deck", 2026-07):** NVDA's July non-deal-roadshow deck (company IR, no rating/PT) lays out the annual-cadence roadmap in one exhibit: **Blackwell/Blackwell Ultra (2024, HBM3e, NVLink 5 @1800 GB/s, Grace CPU, BlueField-3, Spectrum5/CX8 51T 800G) → Rubin / Rubin Ultra on the Oberon rack (2026, HBM4→HBM4e + custom HBM & die stacking, NVLink 6→7 Switch @3600 GB/s, Vera CPU, BlueField-4, Spectrum6/CX9 102T CPO 1600G, NVL72/NVL144) → Feynman on the Kyber rack (2028, NVLink 8 CPO, Rosa CPU, BlueField-5, Spectrum7/CX10 204T CPO, NVL576/NVL1152)** — NVFP4 the numerics thread throughout (NVIDIA "NDR deck", 2026-07). Note the deck's public cadence puts **Rubin/Rubin Ultra in 2026 and Feynman/Kyber in 2028** — a company-stated framing to reconcile against the sell-side/SemiAnalysis debate above (Rubin big customer ramp Q4/2H26; Kyber slipping to ≥2028). **GPU pricing durability (Source: SemiAnalysis, in the deck):** H100 AI-cloud GPU pricing **+13% trailing-3mo / +21% trailing-6mo**; A100 **+7% trailing-3mo / +5% trailing-6mo** — durability drivers management cites: large/growing installed base (cloud/on-prem/edge), fungibility across AI/non-AI & training/inference, and CUDA-driven continuous perf gains extending useful life. **Performance/inference economics:** MLPerf Training v6.0 — won every benchmark, only platform to submit all tests; **Blackwell Ultra GB300 up to 1.6x faster than GB200**; **GB300 NVL72 throughput up to 2.7x in a single round through software alone**; record reasoning throughput **2.5M tokens/sec (DeepSeek-R1, offline)**; on SemiAnalysis InferenceX, GB300 NVL72 delivers **50x higher throughput per MW, 35x lower cost/token vs Hopper (agentic/low-latency), 1.5x lower cost/token vs GB200 on long-context** (NVIDIA "NDR deck", 2026-07; perf sources: SemiAnalysis InferenceX / MLPerf). **Agents:** GB300 NVL72 up to **20x more agents per MW / 20x better agentic-coding performance vs H200** (measured on DeepSeek V4 Pro, real coding-agent trajectories across 12+ languages), already in production with **Baseten, DeepInfra, Together AI (e.g. Cursor on Blackwell)** (Artificial Analysis AgentPerf, per the deck). **Vera CPU:** 1.5x overall vs latest-gen 128-core x86, 1.6x vs prior-gen Grace, sustained 90% of peak memory bandwidth (highest of any CPU tested) and **4x memory-bandwidth/core vs x86** (Phoronix, per deck); Nemotron 3 Ultra frontier intelligence at **~5x faster output / ~30% lower cost-to-task-completion vs best open models** (Artificial Analysis, per deck). **Ecosystem flywheel:** **7,000+ apps/models/libraries on CUDA**; GitHub (Apr 2026) shows **1.4B commits (~3x), 90M PRs merged, 20M new repos/month**, ~30-40M developers (~$3T salaries) producing ~3x output (≈$9T productivity); token generation growing exponentially (OpenRouter tokens/month chart rising to ~123T+). Read-through: this is company (not sell-side) roadmap/positioning color — additive to the wiki's existing roadmap, pricing-durability (cf. the 06-27 A100 spot +20% / 07-01 JPM rental datapoints) and tokens-per-watt / AI-factory-ROI bull threads; no house estimate superseded. [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NDR_July2026_.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NDR_July2026_.html)
- **China H200 re-opening — Beijing easing (The Information / Qianer Liu, 2026-07-08 EXCLUSIVE; @jukan05, 2026-07-08; DIGITIMES, 2026-07-09):** China plans to let **ByteDance, Alibaba and DeepSeek buy a LIMITED number of H200** to offset a domestic AI-chip shortage (two people with direct knowledge); @jukan05 quantifies it as **~200,000 H200 approved for training**. Trump had granted NVDA December permission to ship H200 to approved Chinese customers, but Beijing had withheld local-purchase approval — protecting domestic chipmakers — until now. DIGITIMES reads the rumored limited reopening within Beijing's broader self-reliance drive. **Read-through:** a possible *partial* re-open of the China DC-compute leg that management still carries at **$0 in the outlook** (see Guide) — upside optionality, not yet in any estimate; whether imports actually clear is the watch item (see Catalysts China line). No house estimate changed.
- **Citi rating/PT + capital-return detail (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-08, Buy, PT $300 @ 28x C27E ~$11.9 EPS):** complementing the 07-08 IR-call color logged in the intra-quarter table, Citi carries **Buy, PT $300** (28x C27E EPS ~$11.9) and adds from the same IR call that NVDA **sticks to the mid-70s% GM guide**; **targets returning ~50% of cash flow to shareholders this year** and expects to **increase buybacks** (the recent **$25B debt offering was mostly for flexibility**); and that on **d-Matrix, "no official collaboration announced."** Reiterates NVDA as its top buy-rated mega-cap DC-semis pick on strong DRAM access amid tight supply. (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-08)
- **NVDA–d-Matrix strategic partnership (The Information, 2026-07-08):** NVDA struck a new strategic partnership with would-be competitor **d-Matrix** (Parasail to combine NVDA infra with d-Matrix accelerators) — framed as Jensen adapting to a world with more chip competitors. Note the tension with Citi's same-day IR read that "no official collaboration announced" on d-Matrix (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-08). (The Information, 2026-07-08)
- **China H200 reopening — sell-side sizing (Redburn, 2026-07-09):** sizing the rumored China H200 reopening (see the 07-08 China bullet above), Redburn pegs it at **~$7bn revenue ≈ ~2% of its FY27 datacenter revenue estimate ($372bn)** — a small positive on easing US-China frictions, not transformational. (Redburn, 2026-07-09)
- **BofA CY2030 revenue framing (@The_AI_Investor citing BofA, 2026-07-08):** BofA now expects NVDA revenue to reach **~$1T in CY2030**, implying **24.2GW of datacenter at ~$31.3B per GW**. (@The_AI_Investor, 2026-07-08)
- **MS NDR highlights — new 3-segment growth framework + capital-return pivot to "value name" (Morgan Stanley · Joseph Moore "NVDA NDR highlights", 2026-07-10):** MS hosted an NDR in California with Jensen Huang / Colette Kress / IR head Toshiya Hari; the team described "accelerating growth rates" even as revenue approaches ~$100bn/qtr, and **reiterates OW / Top Pick in semis, PT $288** (bull $330 @ ~23x bull MW CY27 EPS $14 / base $288 @ ~22x MW CY27 EPS $13.08 / bear $160 @ ~16x bear CY27 EPS $10; price ref $202.78, 07/09). MS ModelWare estimates: **GAAP revenue $216B(26)/$393B(27e)/$599B(28e)/$784B(29e)** (growth 82.0% 2026, 52.4% 2027), **GM 71.3%(26)→74.4%(27e)→72.5%(28e)→72.0%(29e)**, **MW EPS $4.61/$8.96/$13.08/$17.63** (consensus-methodology EPS $4.77/$8.98/$13.15/$17.69). NVDA framed growth around the **new 3-segment structure**: (1) **AI labs ~20% of total demand** — one of the more prominent frontier models was built mostly on an ASIC so NVDA had minimal exposure, but NVDA share there has since risen to **~50%**, while other frontier models remain mostly on NVDA; (2) **traditional hyperscalers ~half of revenue** — increasingly capped by land/space & power, but SAM expanding via networking and now CPU (repeated the **$20B CPU target this year**, with **~half of that outside the head node** = a substantial standalone-CPU-rack presence with **Vera in 2H**, purpose-built for single-threaded workloads with a large die, smaller core counts, AI-optimized memory); (3) **"AI clouds, industrial & enterprise" ~half of DC revenue** — sovereign/reshoring-driven, minimal competition, fragmented but large-scale (retail/finance/biotech, regional AI capacity). MS view: **both NVDA & AVGO AI businesses grow 80%+ next year and are supply-constrained → no dramatic ASIC-vs-GPU share shift**; contacts substantiate that **the lowest cost per token is frequently from NVDA** (cheaper silicon ≠ better token economics), and NVDA's compute share climbed 2024→2026. **Product roadmap tracking:** a strong Vera Rubin cycle drives the next 12mo; on the "Rubin Ultra slips to 2028" stories the company said that is **not true — Rubin Ultra ships next year**, but acknowledged form-factor changes as the **Kyber rack is being replaced by a "better solution"** potentially supporting a **larger single-rack scale-up domain** (presented as a positive); **800V + optical scale-up between racks remain on schedule**. **Memory shortage** looks to last several years — NVDA cut **LPDDR5 content per rack in half** to meet rack demand; compute/networking/memory are inter-related and NVDA can use two to optimize a shortage of the third; management highlighted **Groq SRAM** ("used to be much more expensive than DRAM"). **Capital return / value pitch:** with **50%+ of cash flow moving to cash return starting now**, MS argues NVDA is "a very clear value name amid large caps" — this NDR was explicitly designed to **broaden the investor base to value-centric investors** (closing the value gap vs. compute-semis peers). Also flagged the new **neocloud business model** (co-investment + credit assurance + revenue-sharing) — investors have focused on the credit-backstop downside, but the **revenue-sharing** upside could make NVDA a **partial owner of a large network of GPU clouds** — and **"nemotron"** open-model development that lets enterprise customers own the full stack for app-specific AI (e.g. circuit design). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NVIDIA_20260710_0236.html)
- **Merchant-CPU economics + content-per-GW ladder (BofA "Core Wars Q2 Preview", Vivek Arya, 2026-07-13; reit Buy, PO $350):** BofA's server-CPU preview reaffirms **Buy / PO $350** (26x CY27E PE ex-cash) and adds CPU-specific colour: NVDA's **Vera CPU ≈ $20bn of sales in F2H27, split roughly 50/50 between head-node (compute-rack) and standalone agentic sockets**; Grace has shipped ~2.5mn units to date and Vera could reach **~4-5mn units in just its first two quarters at a ~$4-5k ASP**; NVDA's **$200bn CPU TAM counts only agentic/RL workloads, not general-purpose x86**. Content-per-GW keeps rising gen/gen — **~$40bn/GW (Blackwell Ultra) → $60-80bn/GW (Rubin/Rubin Ultra) → ~$100bn/GW (Feynman)** — as NVDA adds chips/racks each generation (Vera Rubin now in full production = 7 chips across 5 racks, lowest token cost). Vera stays behind on raw core count (88 Olympus cores vs 192-256 x86) but NVDA's extreme co-design + Spatial-Multi-Threading offsets it, and BofA argues the CPU-GPU ratio trends toward ~1:1 across both racks. Mizuho (Vijay Rakesh, 2026-07-11) separately carries **NVDA Outperform, PT $300** (~25x F28E EPS; from the ~$215 context in its 06-03 DELL note) — GPU/ASIC leader + technology-enabler of the AI-DC-capex ramp to **$1.2T (2027E) / >$1.4T (2028E)** ($50B/GW, 106GW 2026-30E ≈ $5.3T). JPM's proprietary server-CPU model (Hung/Hariharan, 2026-07-12) frames **NVDA's Vera as the key driver of the ARM server-CPU wave** (ARM ~90% of AI head-node CPUs by 2028; global AI-accelerator shipments 7.6mn 2024 → 32.5mn 2028, ~50% CAGR; accel-to-CPU ratio 4:1 → 2:1 → ~1:1). [BofA source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Vivek_on_CPUs.html) · [Mizuho source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/DC_Capex_Ramping_to__14T_in_2028E_FCF_Outlook_Improving.html) · [JPM source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Server_JPM_proprieta_2026-07-12_5362742.html)
- **NDR audio recap — new datapoints beyond the 07-10 written note (Morgan Stanley · Moore "NVDA NDR recap", 2026-07-14):** MS's audio recap of the same-week Jensen/Colette NDR adds specifics not in the 07-10 highlights note. **Timing is itself the tell** — NDRs are normally Oct/Nov near GTC; hosting one now signals Jensen wants to tell a new story and **broaden the shareholder base to value + income investors** (a name with a good FCF yield *and* growth "closing in on triple digits," a combination Moore calls unique for a mega-cap). **Segmentation:** AI labs ~20% of revenue = a couple of frontier LLM developers — NVDA has "very strong share with one and approximately zero with the other," and via recent arrangements can now **ramp with the lab it had been shut out of → potential AI-lab share gains** (the ASIC-built-frontier-model account, cf. the 07-10 "~50%" read). Traditional cloud (4-5 US hyperscalers) ≈ half of revenue, holding strong share vs the "ASIC thread and AMD thread" while expanding TAM into networking + CPUs; the third bucket (AI cloud / enterprise / sovereign-reshoring) has "no competition, no ASICs coming in." **ASIC confidence — NVDA at ~85% revenue share, "a level they can sustain"** (Moore: management much more confident than the market perceives; the Computex chart argues NVDA has the highest tokens-per-GW, fastest bring-up, longest meantime-between-failure and **longest asset life** vs a single-purpose ASIC — "the stock market gives ASICs too much credit"). **Vera CPU $20B decomposed:** ~$10B head-node + ~$2B other AI-rack content (storage etc.) + **~$8B standalone server** (mostly 2H since Vera only shipped mid-year, so the run-rate "moves significantly higher"); Moore has spoken to **>1 customer buying >$1B of standalone Vera this year**; Vera = low-core-count / big-die / beefy single-threaded cores for agentic, *not* directly threatening Intel/AMD's multi-threaded cloud workloads (AMD pushes back). **Memory:** multi-year shortage but not a hard bottleneck — "token growth 10X vs memory growth 20%" so NVDA optimizes across compute/networking/storage/memory rather than waiting (LPDDR5 nerfing); trims the *peak* earnings power for memory makers vs the crisis feel of 3 months ago but is durable — Jensen: **"your Micron stock will be fine."** On GM, mgmt reiterates mid-70s around this product cycle (HBM4 re-pricing ~a year out the risk), but **MS models NVDA gross margin down to 73% next year** (deliberately conservative). **Kyber — development STOPPED:** Ruben Ultra "very much on track for next year," but **Kyber is giving way to a different technology — NVDA has stopped Kyber development and is "doing something better,"** a superior scale-up domain read as **>576 die/rack** (die-per-package also shifting 4→2); **800V (Rubin) and CPO optical scale-out (Rubin Ultra) both remain on track**; Jensen: "if we had never talked about Kyber, we would just change the name." **Neo Cloud revenue-sharing (new model):** per a Kress-co-authored blog, NVDA finances the initial neocloud capex + lends (CoreWeave-style), then **collects ~half the gross margin once the cloud is running and recognizes it as 100% gross-margin revenue** — effectively an NVDA jointly-owned CSP network built entirely on NVDA hardware (a ~1yr-out, divisive-but-high-margin optionality). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_joe_moore_14_jul_26.html)
- **Hyperscale capex to $1.2T (2027) / $1.4T (2028) — NVDA the priciest platform per GW (Morgan Stanley · Nowak "Hyperscale capex to $1.4T", 2026-07-14):** in MS Internet's Tech-Talk recap of its aggregate hyperscale-capex raise (to ~$1.2T in 2027 / ~$1.4T in 2028, +15-30% over 2 yrs on memory-price inflation + a first bottoms-up 2028 chip/volume build done with Joe Moore's team), Nowak pegs **NVDA infra at ~$40 → ~$50bn per GW over time** vs **~$15-25bn/GW for custom silicon (Trainium/TPU)** — the widest per-GW gap in the note, but read alongside the capex raise as directly capex-additive for NVDA. Company GW trajectories: **Meta ~3.5(25)→8+(26)→14(27)→21(28)GW; Amazon ~14(25)→36(28)GW; Google ~5(25)→30+(28)GW — Google adds the most compute of all.** (Morgan Stanley · Nowak "Hyperscale capex to $1.4T", 2026-07-14) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_brian_nowak_on_hyperscale_capex_raise_to_1_4t_new_.html)
- **Jensen personal damage-control on the delay rumors (@aleabitoreddit, 2026-07-14):** Huang went out directly to rebut the delay narrative — **800V and rack-to-rack optical interconnects "progressing fully in line with the original plan with no material delay"**; **Rubin Ultra NOT slipping to 2028 — "on track to ship next year"**; the Kyber→new-design swap is "an architectural optimization, not impacting delivery timelines." On competition: **"no loss of market share" — a leading frontier lab that previously relied heavily on internal ASICs has shifted nearly 50% of its compute to NVDA.** On hyperscaler-reliance: AI frontier labs ~20% of demand, traditional clouds also buy Vera CPUs + high-speed networking, plus sovereign AI. This is the **first direct-from-CEO** corroboration of the roadmap-intact read the sell-side had been asserting (NVDA IR 07-07 denial · Citi 07-08 IR · MS 07-10 NDR · BofA/Fenske 07-12). (@aleabitoreddit, 2026-07-14)
- **China H200 — first shipments actually begin, but tiny and buyer list halved (Reuters / FT / Hedgeye / @jukan05, 2026-07-14):** a top US official says H200 shipments to China **"have begun"** (Hedgeye), though Reuters reports **"very few"** chips have actually shipped and names **ZTE among the licensed Chinese buyers**. Countervailing: **FT — "Nvidia halves Asia buyer list in China chip crackdown,"** and per FT/@jukan05 Beijing's approval for *broad* local sales is still not granted (large-scale sales "remains low"; domestic computing capacity insufficient; domestic chipmakers "completely sold out"). Read-through: the China DC-compute leg management still carries at **$0 in the outlook** is now **partly real but very limited** — first flow started, gated by a narrowed approved-buyer list; the 07-09 Redburn ~$7bn (~2% of FY27 DC rev) sizing remains the upper-bound frame, not yet in any estimate. (Reuters/FT/Hedgeye/@jukan05, 2026-07-14)
- **Nokia–NVIDIA AI-RAN platform commercial from 2027 (Nokia/NVIDIA via @QQ_Timmy, 2026-07-15):** the first commercial co-developed AI-driven RAN platform goes on sale from 2027 — carriers move **+50% more data on the same spectrum in 2027, ~2x by 2028** (20% today), Open-RAN-compatible and software-upgradable to 6G, with a software-subscription revenue model on top of hardware. Jensen frames it as "a generational shift bringing CUDA and AI into baseband," turning the RAN into a global-scale AI computer. Flows from the **Oct-2025 strategic partnership (NVDA's $1B Nokia investment)** — a new edge/telecom demand leg for NVDA silicon, small near-term but strategically aligned with the physical-AI/edge story. (Nokia/NVIDIA via @QQ_Timmy, 2026-07-15)
- **Blackwell wafer orders increasing + CoWoS mix (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17):** wafer supply is still very tight and TSMC **"super hot" (rush) runs remain heavily used**; Jefferies sees **Nvidia increasing its Blackwell wafer orders at TSMC** — a positive for TSMC 2H26 gross margin (super-hot runs are GM-accretive). On packaging mix, Nvidia's **flagship GPUs use TSMC's CoWoS-L**; **some products use CoWoS-S/R**, which — given TSMC's CoWoS-S/R capacity constraint — are increasingly outsourced to OSATs (**ASE, Amkor**); the transcript names "Nvidia's V100" as a CoWoS-S example (attributed as stated). Read-through: incremental Blackwell demand into a still-tight supply chain, corroborating the persistent-shortage thread on this page (Jefferies · Sherman Chang, 2026-07-17). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_jefferies_update_with_fubon_post_tsmc_2q_earnings.html)
- **🆕 Feynman is the lead CoPoS product — the packaging platform behind the 2028+ roadmap, sized (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Timothy Arcuri et al, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership? — Intel's EMIB-T vs TSMC's CoPoS", 2026-07-27).** UBS's advanced-packaging deep-dive is a TSMC/Intel note, but it puts NVDA first in line on the next packaging generation. **The core call: "TSMC will likely start CoPoS ramp with 310x310mm² panels for the first generation, with mass production targeted for 2028. We believe Nvidia's Feynman may be the first CoPoS client in H228-2029, followed by AMD and ASICs in 2029-30"** — and UBS's whole TSMC share model is built "assuming CoPoS enters mass production for Nvidia in 2028, and for others in 2029-30." So Nvidia is both the anchor tenant and the timing gate for the CoPoS transition; TSMC could later enhance scalability by migrating to larger 510x515mm² panels. **What CoPoS buys the roadmap:** UBS frames it as "a full-generation upgrade to CoWoS rather than merely a packaging form-factor transition" — stronger package-level performance via tighter interconnect and better signal integrity, plus "broader integration options such as embedded capacitors, **3D stacking** and eventually **co-packaged optics (CPO)**" — i.e. the packaging substrate for exactly the CPO-NVSwitch-at-Feynman thread already on this page. **The near-term CoWoS ceiling, quantified from the same note:** separately from CoPoS, "TSMC has added **14x-reticle CoWoS** to its roadmap, with support for around **10 large compute dies and 20 HBM stacks**… slated for production in **2028**, with the potential to scale beyond 14x reticle in 2029" (_this is the 14x-reticle **CoWoS** spec, not a CoPoS spec — attribute precisely_); UBS notes "scaling CoWoS beyond 10x reticle remains challenging" and that CoPoS is the better answer for larger packages. On the competing platform, UBS thinks **Intel's EMIB-T could hold an edge on ultra-large packages (12-14x reticle or larger)** because it avoids an ultra-large interposer — relevant to whichever path Feynman-class packages ultimately take. **Why the alternatives exist at all — the supply backdrop:** "due to **persistent CoWoS capacity constraints at TSMC in recent years**, other advanced packaging solutions from competing foundries and OSATs are gaining traction," with EMIB-T "emerging as a competitive alternative given TSMC's supply constraints and growing interest in supply-chain diversification" among Nvidia, AMD and Broadcom. That is the same CoWoS-allocation squeeze the page tracks via Nomura (06-30) and Jefferies (07-17), now stated as the structural driver of platform fragmentation. **Substrate supply — the NVDA BOM read:** UBS puts **Ibiden's current market share of ABF substrate in NVIDIA Blackwell / Blackwell Ultra GPUs at 70-80%**, on a track record including CoWoS-L and demonstrated warpage control; it expects Ibiden to hold that position through the CoPoS transition because "reliability and manufacturing track record are likely to outweigh cost considerations, **given ABF package costs represent only a small portion of the overall bill of materials for GPU/HBM**" — i.e. a genuine single-supplier concentration, but on a low-value BOM line, and UBS expects customers to pursue multi-sourcing anyway given the anticipated shortage of high-end ABF substrates. Among Taiwan substrate makers UBS names **Unimicron** a key CoPoS beneficiary (with Nan Ya PCB expanding too); Unimicron is also a potential second source on Intel's EMIB-T if volumes surprise. **Platform-level TAM:** advanced-packaging industry revenue **US$19.0bn (2026E) → US$36.3bn ('27) → US$58.1bn ('28) → US$87.1bn ('29) → US$122.0bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR**, with TSMC modeled at 84%/82% share in 2026-27 falling to **65-70% by 2030** in UBS's stress test (62% in the lower case) as Intel's EMIB-T takes 13-18%. ⚠️ **No NVDA rating or price target in this note** — UBS's disclosure page lists **NVIDIA Corp at US$208.76 (23 Jul 2026)** as an additional-price reference only; the page's existing PT cluster (MS $288, BofA $350, Barclays $275, DB $255, UBS house $280-285/Arcuri) is untouched. (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Timothy Arcuri et al, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_adv_packaging.html)

## Capstone estimates (house model)
_Source: Capstone official model (`Modelo Felipe NVDA.xlsx`, 2026-06-17). Model years 2025/26/27 (FY ≈ CY). DC ~90%+ of revenue._

| | 2025 | 2026E | 2027E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| Revenue ($bn) | 216 | 407 | 661 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;— y/y | — | +89% | +62% |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;— Data Center | 194 | 379 | 629 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;— Compute / Networking | 162 / 31 | 313 / 67 | 518 / 111 |
| EPS — GAAP / non-GAAP ($) | 4.90 / 4.55 | 9.26 / 9.30 | 15.44 / 15.49 |
| Gross margin | ~71% | ~75% | ~74% |
| Op margin | ~89% | ~92% | ~94% |
| **GW sold/yr** (DC-rev implied) | **~9.2** | **~16.0** | **~24.8** |
| **Cumulative GW (installed base)** | ~8.6 | ~19.0 | ~35.1 |
| Revenue / GW ($bn) | ~21 | ~24 | ~25 |
| Chips deployed (k) | 4,731 | 7,670 | 7,570 |
| &nbsp;&nbsp;— Blackwell / Rubin | 4,055 / 0 | 6,570 / 1,100 | 1,170 / 6,400 |

**House read:** the model sells **~9 → 16 → 25 GW/yr** of DC compute (cumulative install base ~9 → 19 → **35 GW** by 2027E) at **~$21–25bn of NVDA revenue per GW** — the Rubin ramp (1.1k → 6.4k k-units) drives the 2027 step even as Blackwell rolls off. Cross-checks vs JPM's $25–35bn NVDA capture/GW (AI Capex 2.0) and the ~$24–25k rev/GW the house uses elsewhere.

## Debate / thesis
- **Capstone model (house view):** see the **Capstone estimates** table above — rev $216B→$407B→$661B (25/26/27E), GAAP EPS $4.90→$9.26→$15.44, **~9→16→25 GW sold/yr** (~35 GW cumulative by '27E). (Capstone official model, 2026-06-17)
- **Bull:** Joe Moore (MS) — **Top Pick since March**, OW PT $288 (briefing 2026-06-03; 2026-06-16 bus-tour call; **post-NDR reiteration 2026-07-10**, PT $288 / bull $330 / bear $160, now pitched as "a very clear value name amid large caps" with 50%+ of cash flow going to cash return and the NDR designed to broaden into value investors — see Current state for the 3-segment framework): "definitive best product on the market" shipping in weeks, best token-per-watt / longest asset life / best AI-factory ROI; trades **mid-teens on next year** vs speculative AI names that have outperformed — value setup, multiple-compression-elsewhere thesis. Arcuri (UBS, 2026-05-21 recap call): supply (PCs + inventory) at **$145B**; purchase-commitment build all pushed to FY28/29 → enough supply to do **~$130B revenue exiting the year**, well above Street; GM mid-70s defensible into FY28 even as BOM shifts to ~2x-pricier HBM4E (possible HBM consignment helps). DC business **out-grew hyperscale capex for 5 straight quarters**. Vera CPU genuinely incremental (agentic harnesses run on CPU). Briefings: Jensen "2H next year so much bigger than this year" (dnystedt 2026-06-09); memory shortage to "persist several years." Power-roadmap math is a tailwind to per-GPU and per-rack content (JPM AI Datacenters & Power, 2025-11-04): dollar-content-per-watt roughly doubles to $0.4-0.5 in VR/Kyber racks. BG2 panel (2026-06) reinforces the demand-sustainability bull: monetization-per-GW *rising* (not deflating) into 2026, ">200B inference exiting the year," and ASIC competition "disappointing" (Meta/Microsoft programs underwhelming; even OpenAI's chip runs hot, needing more cooling/power) — Jensen's $1T-two-years-ago call now looks low. JPM agentic-demand read (2026-06-16): Google now processes ~3.2 quadrillion tokens/month (~7x y/y), enterprises blowing through full-year AI budgets in months, per-token cost falling 60-70%/yr but usage compounding faster (Jevons) — the binding constraint is power, which favors NVDA's tokens-per-watt. Fubon (2026-06-05) checks: NVDA still ~50%+ of TSMC CoWoS allocation in 2027, total NVDA GPU production "could reach 10mn+ in 2027"; Vera CPU moves to ASE FoCoS packaging (outside the CoWoS allocation). **BofA Vivek Arya — reit Buy, PO raised to $350 (2026-07-07; prior PO $320, 2026-05-19 → Changelog)**; addresses the 4 standing debates (HBM cost, ASIC, crowded ownership, vendor financing), argues HBM content/rack rises only ~$0.2-0.3mn Blackwell→Rubin vs +$2-3mn rack pricing (pricing power wins), NVDA holds 65-70%+ AI-capex share, and the 7-yr-low 18x fwd P/E already implies an unjustified ~30-35% CY27/28 EPS cut. At the earlier 05-19 setup call (PO $320) he had sees buy-side FY27 (cal-26) EPS in the **low-to-mid-teens ($13-15)** vs sell-side ~$8.50, and cal-27 buy-side ~20% above the ~$1T 3-yr (cal25-27) DC consensus, with bulls toward **~$600B 2027 DC vs ~$500B sell-side**. Arya's framework: ~30-40% of the market can't design its own silicon → NVDA is the only choice there; of the remaining ~60% (hyperscaler ASIC + merchant), NVDA holds ~half the units → **~70% of sector economics on ~50%+ of units**. He flags NVDA at ~24x forward (lowest in a decade) but warns **positioning** is a headwind (>8% of S&P, the level where AAPL/MSFT made relative peaks) and pushes capital returns as the durability signal (dividend yield 0.02% → could go 40-100bps; cash returns only ~47% of FCF vs ~80% for the peer group) — expects a buyback/dividend step-up more likely on the July call. On the agentic CPU:GPU-ratio debate, Arya is dismissive ("pure BS"): the intelligence-cluster CPU:GPU ratio (1 Grace : 2 Blackwell; 1 Axion : 2 TPU) hasn't changed; agentic CPU racks are a *separate* room, and CPUs at $2-4k vs accelerators at 10-20x make unit-ratio comparisons meaningless. UBS Arcuri (2026-06-15) reiterates the house "**over on earnings, under on the multiple**" frame — fundamentally nothing bad to say, but a persistently low multiple; the Anthropic deal helps by showing growth accruing to model-builders that plows back into capex.
- **Bear:** Vital Knowledge / Crisafulli (briefing 2026-06-11): low PE = market doubting **demand sustainability** — Oracle F27 capex/sales >100%, hyperscalers can't generate FCF, "picks-and-shovels earning a fortune but PEs low"; price war in a capital-intensive industry is "toxic." Barclays/Rand (2026-06-15): NVDA still the **"popular funding short"**, persistently lagging SOX/AMD, "hard to find a high-conviction catalyst." Chanos (2026-06-13) needling the GPU-DC cost economics. Structural ASIC threat sharpening: **AWS Trainium3** now in GA with Trainium4 announced (AWS Trainium3 Deep Dive, 2025-12-04) — N3P node (alongside Vera Rubin), HBM3E 12-high, scale-up bandwidth 2x Trn2 via PCIe Gen6, switched-fabric rack ("Amazon Basics GB200"), and an aggressive open-source software push (NKI compiler, PyTorch/JAX/XLA) explicitly aimed at eroding the CUDA moat from the developer side; SemiAnalysis frames it as a third front (with Google TPUv7 and AMD MI450X) Jensen "must now contend with," though they still expect NVDA to "stay King of the Jungle" if it keeps accelerating. JPM has AI **ASIC volume CAGR 32% vs GPU 7% (2025-28)**; UBS sees custom ASICs reaching **~25-30% of total accelerator TAM** long-term. JPM "AI Capex 2.0" (2026-06-16) sharpens the ASIC mix call: ASIC unit share of accelerators 42% (2026E) → **53% (2027E)** — i.e. ASICs out-shipping merchant GPUs by 2027 — with hyperscaler in-house silicon at ~30-40% TCO advantage (no merchant margin, cheaper Ethernet, Google Ironwood ~2x perf/watt of Trillium; AWS Trainium3 ~30% better TCO/FP8-PFLOP vs GB300 NVL72), Broadcom AI revenue ~$60B (2026) → **$150B+ (2027)**, and frontier models now trained/served on ASICs (Anthropic on >1M Trainium2 + TPU; Gemini on TPU). SemiAnalysis (2026-06-09): CUDA moat still intact at Day-0 (NVDA's TensorRT-LLM actually buggy on DeepSeek V4, but native CUDA SGLang/vLLM carried it), yet Huawei CANN now also delivers Day-0 inference — a second working stack where last year only CUDA worked, and Kimi K2.6 beats NVDA's own Nemotron 3 Ultra on coding. Arcuri's caveat that NVDA is still correlated to y/y revenue growth which is **peaking ~FQ2** → potential sentiment headwind. China DC TAM structurally impaired (Huawei 80%+ stack target, export controls). **Incremental-share math (BofA Vivek Arya, 2026-05-19):** on sell-side numbers, NVDA's 2027 *incremental* DC sales (~$150B of the +$240B sector add: NVDA +$150B / AVGO +$60B / AMD +$30B) imply **<70% incremental share** — the arithmetic source of the "share-loss" narrative; NVDA pushed back ("no way our share is coming down"), which Arya reads as a tell the real 2027 number is >$500B. Tim Rohde (BofA, AVGO callback, 2026-06-04): if forced to pick, **prefers NVDA over AVGO** — NVDA's multiple has already collapsed while AVGO's "still has more to come down" (partly the Google/MediaTek drumbeat), and NVDA has more degrees of freedom (drops a $20B Vera CPU program "out of thin air" and gets supply) vs AVGO's lumpier, more concentrated, backend/substrate-constrained book; reiterates a durable ~70/30 GPU/ASIC split and that share matters little in a growing market where NVDA is *gaining* at Anthropic. Blaine (Citi/Jefferies Asia recal, 2026-05-22): NVDA remains a "funding short" — story not broken, but buy-side sees bigger inflections elsewhere; ~$15+ EPS power widely known and the stock "too cheap," but hard to see it work absent further upside vs Asia-supply-chain checks that came in ~in-line-to-below.
- **Depreciation-as-a-lever critique (Michael Burry / "Cassandra Unchained", 2026-07-09, bear):** Burry argues hyperscaler depreciation is an economic lever, not a physical measure — **AMZN shortened a subset of server lives to 5yr (from 6yr), adding ~$677mn of expense over 9 months, while META extended useful server lives to 5.5yr, reducing its depreciation hit to net income by ~$2.9bn** — same hardware, opposite conclusions; the frontier moves faster than hyperscaler/neocloud depreciation schedules. A read-through risk to the durability of the AI-infra earnings that underpin NVDA demand (distinct from his vaguer 07-08 "Short Thoughts" note logged in the intra-quarter table). (Michael Burry / "Cassandra Unchained", 2026-07-09)
- **Morgan Stanley (Stephen Byrd) stays constructive on AI infrastructure via Jevons Paradox; Blackwell→Feynman −75% token cost (2026-06-26).** MS's Head of Thematic Research discusses the "tokenmaxxing" pullback and explicitly argues it aligns with Jevons Paradox — as token costs fall (~75% estimated from Blackwell to Feynman generations), compute demand explodes rather than contracts. The token-spend optimization by enterprises (Uber, Meta, Amazon, Microsoft) is framed as "a natural, healthy phase of early adoption" and an optimization, not a demand collapse. "The underlying trend of falling compute costs driving increased utility and demand remains intact." MS remains constructive on AI infrastructure providers (semiconductors, cloud hyperscalers). Bull (Morgan Stanley / Stephen Byrd "Discussion on tokenmaxxing", 2026-06-26). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_26_stephen_byrd_on_tokenmaxxing.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_26_stephen_byrd_on_tokenmaxxing.html)
- **Bull (token-optimization read-through):** UBS stays **bullish on infra/semis demand** through the enterprise "token-maxxing" scare — token budgets aren't unlimited, but "the bulk of capex is ultimately driven by the frontier model providers and we have yet to see the wave of Blackwell trained (and inferenced) models — this should present much better token economics." Multimodal (audio/video/physical AI) adds upside bias to infra demand, and more open models / down-tiering / demand for specialized real-time-token infra (e.g. coding where speed is the main consideration) all read as volume-positive. The **caveat**: UBS comes out "neutral... token optimization could impact frontier model API revs growth" and warns **"AI price sensitivity could limit near-term GPU price increases"** — i.e. constructive on volume/demand, a slight check on near-term GPU pricing power (UBS "The Ramp in Token Optimization" / Arcuri-Keirstead, 2026-06-23).
- **Expert-call reads on competitive position (AlphaSense, 2026-06-30/07-01):** a Senior Manager at AMD (7yrs AI server/rack-scale NPI experience) says **NVIDIA currently dominates the GPU market** on strong vendor relationships and early tech investment, but competitors are catching up — could lead to more balanced competition; supply-chain shortages persist in memory, storage, power infrastructure and cables but should ease as investment/yields improve; co-packaged optics and liquid cooling flagged as emerging technologies (AlphaSense expert call, 2026-06-30). Separately, the Founder/Principal Advisor of NEUBoard (ex-AWS SageMaker/Bedrock, ex-Cerebras) sees neocloud providers like CoreWeave as advantaged by close NVIDIA alignment/vertical integration but exposed to customer concentration and high capex; NVIDIA is seen **maintaining a strong training moat** but facing **rising inference competition from Trainium, AMD, and Cerebras** (AlphaSense expert call, 2026-07-01).
- **BACKFILL / HISTORICAL BASE RATE — how the GB200 NVL72 rack-scale ramp actually went (SemiAnalysis "H100 vs GB200 NVL72 Training Benchmarks — Power, TCO and Reliability Analysis; Software Improvement Over Time", 2025-08-20):** _an August-2025 source added on 2026-07-24 as historical structure — it supersedes nothing on this page and is NOT in the current intra-quarter table. Its value is as the only independent record of a rack-scale ramp curve, i.e. the base rate against which to judge the Vera Rubin ramp._ **Economics at the time:** GB200 NVL72 rack **$3.1M ($3.9M all-in per rack)** vs an H100 server at **~$190k (~$250k all-in)** — **capex/GPU 1.6–1.7x and TCO ~1.6x the H100**, so the rack had to be at least 1.6x faster just to break even; GB200 1200W/chip vs H100 700W. **The curve is the point:** perf/TCO vs H100 was **still below 1.0x in May-2025, reached 1.5x by July-2025, and was projected to 2.7x within 3–6 months** — i.e. a rack-scale platform can ship materially *behind* the prior generation on delivered economics and close the gap almost entirely on software inside two quarters. On DeepSeek 670B BF16, tok/s/GPU went **+30% (May-25) → 2.5x (Jul-25) → projected 4.7x (Dec-25)** at 42.0% MFU, cost/M tokens **$0.684 → $0.307** vs H100's $0.468, and a 670B pretrain **$4.5M (Jul-25) → $2.5M projected (Dec-25)**; power efficiency 2.2x by Jul-25, projected 4x lower J/token by Dec-25 (a 100MW datacenter trains 4x more tokens). **Reliability was the drag, and it was rack-scale-specific:** GB200 NVL72 **MTBI 1,000–3,000 GPU-days vs H100's 2,000–5,000**, **XID 149 errors traced to NVLink copper-backplane signal integrity**, blast radius **72 GPUs vs 8**, compute-tray swap 1–4h, switch-tray swap 1–4h **and it drains the whole rack**, backplane swap **8–24h** — enough to cut the Llama4 Maverick perf/TCO advantage from ~50% cheaper to only 20–30% cheaper once hot-spare/spare-rack cost was counted. At that point NVL72 racks were used **only for inference, small experiments and dev jobs**, neoclouds were choosing **HGX B200/B300 over NVL72**, and SemiAnalysis's verdict was that "**vanilla B200 is better performance TCO than GB200 NVL72 for pretraining normal sized models**," with NVL72 winning as models scale and RL comes in. Read-through for today's debate: the Blackwell rack generation took roughly two quarters of software to earn its price premium and a further period to fix backplane reliability — a concrete prior for the Rubin/Oberon ramp (which stays in the same NVL72 scale-up domain) and a reminder that early-ramp perf/TCO prints are not the steady state. See also the Amphenol Paladin-HD connector read-through now filed on [APH.md](APH.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2025_08_20_ENC_H100_vs_GB200_NVL72_Training_Benchmarks_-_Power_TCO_and_Reliability_Analysis_Software_Improvement_Over_Ti.html)
- **Stale expert-call read-through — flagged Q1'26 vintage (AlphaSense · ex-AWS GenAI BD "AMZN ExpertCall", interview 2026-03-12):** _dated Q1'26 — NOT folded into the current intra-quarter table; carried only as historical context._ NVDA's **design cadence compressed to ~18 months** (from 2-2.5yr); **AWS Trainium ~25-35% cheaper** for the same Anthropic workload (a deliberate margin hedge); NVDA invested **$2bn in Nebius**; Vera Rubin flagged as next. Vintage caveat on the ASIC-cost-gap / cadence debate. (AlphaSense ex-AWS GenAI BD, interview 2026-03-12)
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands:** MS OW $288 Top Pick · UBS (Arcuri) constructive, house bull on supply · DB (Seymore) **Hold PT $255** on valuation (2026-06-02) · UBS house PT $285 (Abrams) · Redburn/Rothschild **Buy** (2026-06-06, dismisses Rubin DRAM "mis-reading") · **Citi (Atif Malik) Buy, top mega-cap DC pick** (2026-07-08, on strong DRAM/HBM access amid constrained supply; IR call reaffirmed Kyber/Rubin Ultra roadmap "fully intact"). On valuation, **UBS 2026 overview** flags multiple expansion as ~done — NVDA tracking ~20x buy-side out-year EPS (~$9-9.50 for C2026), so the stock should ~track EPS rather than re-rate, but still models **40-50% EPS growth in 2027** and says it's "premature to adopt a more Neutral position"; visibility into 2027 unlikely before Spring/GTC. On the CPU $20B number Arcuri cuts it to ~$5B "apples-to-apples" chip equivalent (rest is systems + DRAM), so don't over-extrapolate the TAM. Joe Moore pushes back on the Intel/AMD ratio framing — agentic CPU is a new vector, not GPU-correlated. Broad buy-the-dip consensus post-AVGO selloff; NVDA the "cleaner AI infra name." **BofA sales desk (Brian Fenske, 2026-07-12)** corroborates the cheap-on-both-bases frame: the **buy-side average EPS sits above the sell-side** (only "normal" beats priced in), the stock is "extremely cheap on both buy- and sell-side estimates," and Fenske dismisses the roadmap-delay chatter as "completely noise" — desk commentary, not a rating (Arya's Buy/PO $350 stands). **Arete (Buy, PT $283)** adds the HBM/packaging nuance — Rubin Ultra likely reverts to 384GB HBM4E (from 1TB) + a two-die solution on CoWoS-L constraints (see the 07-21 Intra-quarter row; Arete "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21).
- **Bull-case math vs the "AI bubble" search-trend risk (Barron's, two issues):** the bull case rests on cheap-multiple-on-huge-EPS-growth arithmetic that shows up across three separate Barron's pieces: (1) "Rally in Chips" (Adam Levine, 2026-06-01 issue): EPS grew **$0.33 (FY23) → $4.77 (FY26)**; Street sees **$12.37 by FY28**, putting the stock at **17x P/E** on that basis, with a **PEG <0.6x** (17x P/E ÷ 0.28 PEG per Barron's table) vs the S&P 500's ~1.0x. BofA's Vivek Arya "estimates Nvidia has a durable 70% share of the AI chip market." (2) "It's Not Too Late to Buy Into Nvidia's AI Growth Story" (Al Root, 2026-05-18 issue): the stock trades at **~24x NTM earnings, a 25% discount to its own historical valuation and 5% below the SOX index** — despite normally carrying a 40% premium to SOX; "since the start of 2025, Nvidia has trailed the index by more than 70 percentage points." Root's bull-case ladder: 26x avg-semi multiple → **$290**; reversion to historical premium-to-sector multiple → **$390**; midpoint **$340** (a 50% gain from the then-current level). (3) The explicit **risk flag** in the same Root piece: Google searches for "AI bubble" are **up ~400% y/y (peaking in November)**, and NVDA must beat sales-growth estimates of ~80% to satisfy the Street given an average beat of only ~3% over the prior 3 quarters — i.e. the search-trend/sentiment risk is rising exactly as the bar to beat stays high. Gavin Baker (CIO, Atreides, in the 2026-06-01 issue) frames the read-through skeptically across the sector: "The multiples cannot all be accurate... You have memory makers at low-to-mid-single-digit P/Es, you have Nvidia at a low P/E, you have other accelerator companies at reasonable multiples" — i.e. either NVDA is mispriced cheap or the "reasonable" multiples elsewhere (Cerebras ~280x 2027E per the same issue's Trader column, vs NVDA's sub-17x 2027 P/E) are the anomaly (Barron's, 2026-05-18 / 2026-06-01 issues).
- **Hyperscaler-vs-neocloud segment-reporting narrative fight:** the new disclosure format is itself contested. Bulls read the 47%→55% hyperscaler share shift (Barron's/Levine, 2026-05-25) as evidence of *broadening* demand — ACIE (neoclouds/enterprise/sovereign) is a large, still-underappreciated second growth leg, per Huang's own framing that it's "poorly understood." Bears read the same data the other way: hyperscaler revenue growth (+115% y/y) now **outpaces** the smaller-customer segment (+74% y/y) even as Huang told the Street "we should be growing faster than hyperscale capex" — Barron's calls this "a key point of concern for investors," and the stock's ~3% two-day post-print drop despite the beat is the market's verdict on that specific tension (Barron's/Adam Levine, 2026-05-25). Tigress Research (Strong Buy, PT $425 @ $214.86, 2026-05-27) sits at the bull extreme of the PT range: "Nvidia continues to be the core infrastructure engine of the artificial-intelligence factory era... unmatched full-stack technology leadership and surging AI capital expenditure" (Barron's Research Reports excerpt, 2026-06-01 issue).
- **Valuation cross-check — PT range and PEG (compiled from Barron's coverage, May-June 2026 issues):**

|---|---|---|---|
