This range ($270 low-end average / $290-$390 bull ladder / $425 high-end single estimate) sits **above** the wiki's already-tracked PT cluster (MS $288 / BofA $320 / Barclays $275 / DB $255 / UBS $285) at the top end (Tigress $425) and roughly brackets it at the average ($270) and bull-ladder ($290-$390) levels — no existing PT/rating on this page is superseded; this is an additive cross-check from a different source set (Barron's compiling sell-side + its own analysis) vs the page's primary equity-call sourcing. Gavin Baker's (Atreides) point stands as the tension: "the multiples cannot all be accurate" across the semi/accelerator complex (Barron's, 2026-06-01).

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕⚠️ RUBIN ULTRA HBM CONFIGURATION — FINAL DECISION "BEFORE END-3Q26" (Morgan Stanley / Charlie Chan, 2026-08-10).** The 08-07 UBS spec (HBM4 8-Hi → HBM4E 8-Hi) is the leading case but **not settled**: MS's checks point to **several SKUs** — high-end HBM4e 8-Hi, lower-end HBM4 12-Hi or 8-Hi. **A dated, binary-ish resolution inside the next ~7 weeks that moves [[MU]]/[[SKHYNIX]]/[[SAMSUNG]] bit and ASP forecasts.**
- **🆕🔴 THE $500BN PLATFORM — THE PER-DEAL TEST IS NOW SPECIFIC: is the 25% a GPU-USAGE backstop or a CREDIT guarantee?** Moore states the preference plainly (*"we would prefer a backstop which involves GPU usage rather than pure credit backing"*) and that *"judging 25%, and passivity, **will be assessed one deal at a time**"* (MS / Joseph Moore, 2026-08-11). NVDA's own language is *"residual value based"* and *"limited."* **Watch the first announced platform deal for the structure, and watch the 10-Q's partner-lease-guarantee line — $3.5bn today — as the running scoreboard.**
- **🆕🔴 REVENUE SHARING IS THE NEW UPSIDE LINE ITEM TO MODEL — and nobody has it in numbers yet.** MS: a **35% revenue share could be >$10bn per GW/yr at 100% gross margin**, worth **10%+ to FY29 EPS**. First evidence would be a rev-share disclosure or a new revenue line on the Aug 26 call.
- **🆕⚠️ THE CREDIT SIGNAL IS THE CONTRARY INDICATOR TO TRACK: NVDA CDS at 77bps (+5bps) vs CDX IG 52bps**, and ~0.40pts over Treasuries demanded on NVDA paper (MS TMT desk / WSJ, 2026-08-11). If the equity thesis is "risk moved off our balance sheet," the credit market disagreeing is the tell. **[[AVGO]]'s BofA Credit downgrade to Marketweight on "residual-value risk" from XPV financing is the same signal in the ASIC complex.**
- **🆕 GS's re-rating checklist (2026-08-11), which doubles as a catalyst list:** hyperscaler profitability metrics supporting sustained spend · **measured** capital outlays in support of the financing platforms · reiterated buybacks/dividends. Stock at **14.4x GS CY27E**; GS is **6%/19% above the Street on CY26/CY27**; **+12% in two weeks means the bar is elevated into the print.**
- **The $500bn financing platform — WATCH FOR TERMS.** Per-partner allocations, tenor, pricing and any NVDA first-loss are all undisclosed (NVIDIA Newsroom, 2026-08-10). ⚠️ On the July SK precedent, the disclosure gap persisted; **treat the headline as mobilization capacity until terms print.**
- **Q1 FY27 print, 2026-05-20 (reported after close) — now the reference print for this quarter's flow.** Barron's framed the setup as: consensus ~$190B CY2026 profit projected — would be the best year for any corporation ever per Dow Jones Market Data (prior record: Saudi Aramco ~$160B in 2022) — against a bar of ~80% sales-growth estimates NVDA needed to beat, vs an average beat of only ~3% over the prior 3 quarters (Barron's/Al Root, 2026-05-18 issue). Outcome: beat, but stock fell ~3% over the following two days on the hyperscaler-vs-ACIE growth-mix optics (Barron's/Adam Levine, 2026-05-25 issue) — see Debate.
- **Q2 FY27 print: 2026-08-26** (guided $91B). Watch hyperscale vs hyperscale-capex growth under new segmentation (Arcuri: becomes a debate if NVDA ever under-grows it).
- **TAM upside from >$1T 2027 capex:** Ben Reitzes (Melius, per Barron's/Al Root, 2026-05-18) expects 2027 capex from hyperscalers + Oracle-type buyers to **top $1 trillion**; "[TAM for AI computing] is bigger than anyone thinks." The four large-cap hyperscalers (AMZN/GOOGL/META/MSFT) are expected to spend **~$700B on AI buildout in 2026** (up from ~$500B guided at the start of the year), against combined EBITDA of ~$800B and "essentially no net debt" — Barron's cites this balance-sheet capacity as the reason the capex step-up is financeable rather than a bubble signal. Cross-check vs the wiki's own JPM $575B ('26)/Nomura 32GW('27) capex/build-out figures above — directionally consistent, Melius/Barron's sit toward the higher end of the range.
- **Vera Rubin production ramp Q3 (Oct qtr)** — slope vs GB300 (Colette: too early to call); NVL72 October ramp. Watch unit optics: JPM's 2028 ~25% unit decline is die-count mechanics, not demand (JPM AI Datacenters & Power, 2025-11-04).
- **Kyber rack likely delayed to ≥2028 — Rubin Ultra 2027 stays on Oberon/NVL72 (Jefferies Asia / Conor O'Mara, 2026-06-22; sharpened by SemiAnalysis 2026-07-06):** Jefferies' Sherman/Jacky sees the "Kyber" orthogonal-backplane-PCB rack (which replaces intra-rack cable cartridges), originally targeted for **Rubin Ultra in 2027**, pushed out to **at least 2028** — supply chain now aiming for Kyber on Rubin Ultra in 2028, with challenges still outstanding even on the simplified **2-canister design (18 trays/canister) vs. the original 4-canister**. **Rubin Ultra in 2027 will stick to the Oberon structure (i.e., NVL72).** Supply-chain read-through: if confirmed, AI PCB/CCL TAM cut **~5%/8% in 2027**, with a further **~11%/16% downside in 2028** if it slips past 2028 or is canceled; extended Oberon adoption benefits **copper-cable vendors** (PCB-replacement threat mitigated), and upstream materials (fiberglass cloth/CCL) keep outperforming downstream PCB makers on industrywide tightness. **Update (SemiAnalysis, via CNBC & MS TMT, 2026-07-06):** confirms the **Kyber NVL144 delay (12+ months, to 2028) on PCB-midplane manufacturability**, and adds that **NVL576 is likely delayed/small-volume** (CPO challenges) and the **NVL72x2 back-to-back alternative is cancelled** (CSP pushback). Because **CPO NVSwitch is not available until Feynman**, NVDA has no proven way to grow the scale-up world size for Rubin Ultra — a scale-up-roadmap gap that opens the door for **AMD MI500X** and **GOOGL TPUv8i 'Broadfly'** (see Current state + Risks).
- **GTC (Spring)** — UBS expects this is when the market finally gets 2027 visibility; until then a likely "holding pattern" (UBS 2026 overview).
- **Scale-up / CPO transition** — NVLink doubling to 14.4 Tbit/s/GPU in Rubin pushes copper toward its limit (~2m range); CPO becomes "central to scale-up" beyond Rubin, cutting interconnect energy >50% (Co-Packaged Optics CPO book, 2026-01-02). NVDA's near-term CPO is on scale-out (Quantum/Spectrum); first big scale-up CPO ramp is modeled on Amazon's Trainium4, i.e. a competitor's platform — watch whether NVDA brings scale-up CPO into Rubin Ultra/Kyber.
- Stockholder meeting 2026-06-24. $20B+ benchmark bond (first HG bond in ~5 yrs; 424B5 filed mid-June) — read as AI-capex confidence.
- China: whether any H200 imports actually clear; longer-term Huawei/domestic substitution. **(Update 2026-07-08/09:** Beijing reportedly easing — plans to let ByteDance/Alibaba/DeepSeek buy a limited ~200k H200 for training per The Information/@jukan05; DIGITIMES frames it within China's self-reliance drive.**) (Update 2026-07-14:** first shipments **have begun but "very few"** per Reuters/Hedgeye, with **ZTE among licensed buyers** — yet **FT reports NVDA "halves its Asia buyer list in a China chip crackdown"** and Beijing broad-sale approval is still not granted. So the leg is now *partly* clearing but tiny + gated; domestic substitution sharpening in parallel — Huawei 8,192-GPU super-node at WAIC (07-12) + Dongfang Suanxin's 14nm chip claiming ~33% higher bandwidth than H200 (07-14).**)**
- Supply-chain bottlenecks to monitor: CoWoS (180k/220k wpm by end-27/28E, Jefferies), HVLP4 copper foil / T-glass / PTFE backplane shortages, HBM4E pricing, optical (COHR/LITE locked to 2028), 800V DC pulled into Q3 2026 (and arriving on VR Ultra racks per JPM).

## Risks
- **Demand sustainability / AI-capex ROI** — hyperscaler + neocloud FCF and financing dependence (Crisafulli, Chanos).
- **Customer concentration** — top-5 cloud/hyperscalers ~50%+ of DC revenue; large direct/indirect customer exposure.
- **Supply concentration** — TSMC (N3/N2 + CoWoS), HBM (SK Hynix/Samsung/Micron), advanced packaging are gating; "supply-constrained throughout life of Vera Rubin."
- **Competition** — custom ASIC the sharpest structural threat: GOOG TPUv7, **AWS Trainium3/4** (open-sourcing software to attack CUDA; AWS Trainium3 Deep Dive, 2025-12-04), AVGO/MRVL merchant ASIC, AMD MI400/MI450X (OpenAI ~26GW deal + equity rebate), inference-specialist silicon. JPM ASIC volume CAGR 32% vs GPU 7% (2025-28); UBS ASIC ~25-30% of accelerator TAM long-term.
- **Scale-up roadmap gap for Rubin Ultra (new, 2026-07-06)** — with the **Kyber NVL144 delayed 12+ months to 2028** (PCB-midplane manufacturability), **NVL576 delayed/small-volume**, **NVL72x2 cancelled**, and **CPO NVSwitch not available until Feynman**, NVDA currently has no proven solution to expand the scale-up world size for Rubin Ultra — a window for **AMD MI500X** and **GOOGL TPUv8i 'Broadfly'** to gain scale-up advantages (SemiAnalysis, via CNBC & MS TMT, 2026-07-06). Bear-ish nuance for the scale-up leg of the moat, distinct from the merchant-ASIC-units debate above.
- **Downstream (rack) integration, not upstream silicon, is now the named gate on the next generation (new, 2026-08-03)** — per Arthur's ODM checks on the Fubon call, the goal of **144 GPUs per rack is fixed ("that's for sure")** but the unsolved problems are **heat dissipation, cable design, and the power density of existing datacenters** — because GPU-to-rack connection is **still cable-based**, with a single rack potentially reaching **~1 million watts**. Verbatim: **"Nvidia face a lot of problem, not only from upstream, it's from downstream."** Hon Hai is still submitting competing **two-rack and four-rack proposals**, and the **new Rubin Ultra rack design is not expected until ~the December quarter of 2027** while Rubin racks start shipping this December quarter — against Shang's warning that **"we are already in August, so the timeline is very, very tight to enter production for Ruben Ultra."** This is a distinct risk from the packaging/CoWoS and scale-up-world-size risks above: those are foundry problems, this one sits at the ODM and in customer datacenters (existing power density is a *customer* constraint NVDA cannot engineer away). Complements the page's power ladder (JPM: VR Ultra ~1.5MW / 800V HVDC) by giving the ODM-side reason the design is unfinished. (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), 2026-08-03)
- **🆕 Time-to-power / approved-MW scarcity, now with a regulatory trigger and a price (Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani, "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity", 2026-08-04, read-through — subject is the miner/AI-infra cohort):** **Governor Abbott has directed the PUCT and ERCOT to audit ALL data-center projects in ERCOT's interconnection process, and ERCOT has PAUSED the "Batch Zero" review that was due to publish classification results on 2026-08-07** (queue **474 GW of REQUESTS**, data centers ~90% of new requests — ⚠️ **a queued/requested figure, not energised / IT-load / facility GW; see `_meta/assumptions.md`**). Bernstein: the audit **"throttles speculative data center pipeline and makes genuine sites with development history more valuable"** — **already-approved MW re-rate upward and time-to-power lengthens for new builds**, which is a revenue-*timing* risk for GPUs that need energised halls. The price of it is already visible in Bernstein's Exhibit 2: **NVDA's contract with IREN yields ~$15mn per IT MW vs ~$10mn under Microsoft's contract with the same counterparty (~50% higher; on a gross-MW basis ~$14mn vs ~$11mn — ⚠️ two different bases, do not mix)** — i.e. **NVDA is paying up for scarce approved megawatts.** IREN is not a wiki-covered name. See [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
- **China / export controls** — China DC compute zeroed in guidance; geopolitical and licensing risk.
- **Valuation/sentiment** — "funding short" status; UBS view that multiple expansion is exhausted (~20x buy-side out-year EPS); correlation to decelerating y/y growth as comps peak ~FQ2.
- **"AI bubble" search-trend / sentiment risk** — Google searches for "AI bubble" up ~400% y/y (peaked November); stock trailed the SOX index by >70pp since start of 2025 despite normally trading at a 40% premium to it; bar to beat (~80% sales growth) stays high vs an average ~3% beat over the prior 3 quarters (Barron's/Al Root, 2026-05-18).
- **Hyperscaler-growth-mix optics** — hyperscaler DC revenue (+115% y/y) now outpacing the ACIE/smaller-customer segment (+74% y/y) even as management told the Street NVDA "should be growing faster than hyperscale capex" — stock fell ~3% over two days post-Q1-print despite the beat on this specific tension (Barron's/Adam Levine, 2026-05-25).
- **HBM/memory-wall structural dependency** — Nvidia commands the "lion's share" of HBM demand through 2027 (Rubin Ultra pushes per-GPU HBM to 1TB), and is likely the most profitable HBM vendor in the world by capturing margin on HBM shipped inside its accelerators — but that same concentration is the supply chain's single point of stress (SK Hynix/Samsung/Micron allocation, HBM4 base-die-first risk, pricing step-ups) already flagged above (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall", 2025-08-11).

Arete's 07-21 two-die re-scoping** · **SemiAnalysis (07-24): the HBM4 pin-speed gambit explained — Nvidia raised Rubin's HBM4 pin speed to 10.7 Gbps (above the original JEDEC spec, 40% above AMD's 7.6 Gbps) specifically to close the memory-bandwidth gap to MI455X; it forced memory suppliers to rework HBM4 and pushed out Rubin upstream output, but "as of today these issues have been finally resolved," and "Vera Rubin will still be the first to deliver tokens at scale compared to MI455 Helios" — ✓ on timing, ⚠ on the materially higher-quality HBM bin Nvidia must now buy** | **⚠ nuance** (Rubin unit/demand trajectory reaffirmed bullish into 2027; the **scale-up roadmap gap for Rubin Ultra** was a genuine competitive-risk nuance per SemiAnalysis 07-06, but NVDA's 07-07 denial + the 07-08 Citi IR check + MS's 07-10 NDR + BofA/Fenske's 07-12 desk read + **Jensen's own 07-14 direct rebuttal — now a 5th corroboration, the first straight from the CEO** — largely walk it back). **07-27 adds the first CUSTOMER-side install read (The Information · Phoebe Liu): cloud execs testing early Vera Rubin racks expect them "much easier to install" than Grace Blackwell was in 2025 — "mechanically and thermally, it is now a third generation product" (same 72 GPU / 36 CPU config), NVDA claims 10x tokens/s/W at ≥2x the GB rack price. Offsets logged, not netted: 1.3M components/rack (vs 1.2M GB), 75% more power and hotter, a lower-power but harder-to-set-up cooling system, "a few months" of software adaptation for large clusters, and one analyst claiming thermal-engineering issues set the FIRST batch of VR servers back ~2 months (NVDA: "nothing has changed from what we already announced"). Scope the good news carefully — NVL144 and NVL576 are not in production, and the more powerful 2H27 rack is flagged as the one that "could present new problems."** **07-27 also adds the first packaging-platform read for the generation AFTER Rubin (UBS · Lin/Abrams/Arcuri): TSMC targets CoPoS mass production in 2028 and "Nvidia's Feynman may be the first CoPoS client in H228-2029," with AMD and ASICs only in 2029-30 — CoPoS framed as "a full-generation upgrade to CoWoS," carrying 3D stacking and eventually CPO. Read as ✓ on the CPO-at-Feynman sequencing the page already carries, but note the standing CoWoS capacity constraint is UBS's stated reason the whole industry is diversifying packaging platforms in the first place, and TSMC's 14x-reticle CoWoS (10 compute dies / 20 HBM stacks) also only reaches production in 2028 — i.e. the scale-up-package ceiling does not lift before then.** **07-28 adds the most specific account yet of what replaces Kyber, from the Fubon primary note: Kyber was cancelled/delayed in MARCH/APRIL on MID-PLANE BANDWIDTH ISSUES, it carried 576 compute dies per rack (144 GPUs × 4 compute dies), and Fubon's checks say NVDA still targets the same 576 compute dies in the replacement. Two candidates are live — (a) conservative: stay on the current Oberon rack and build one system from FOUR racks on traditional scale-out (144 compute dies each = 576); (b) a TWO-RACK design Fubon calls "Taycan" — 144 GPUs × 2 compute dies per rack, NPO/CPO for scale-out BETWEEN the two racks, cable retained for scale-up inside. Neither is finalised, and Fubon explicitly refuses to name winners and losers as a result. The one directional call it makes cuts FOR the optics chain: "nVidia is still aggressive in the NPO/CPO solution despite a lot of market noise." Read against the page's standing "CPO NVSwitch not until Feynman" line — Fubon has NPO/CPO doing the inter-rack scale-out job one generation earlier, which is a different claim from CPO in the scale-up domain and should not be netted against it.** **08-03 adds the PRIMARY transcript of that same Fubon call (Sherman Shang / Arthur, hosted by Jefferies), and it moves the open question from silicon to the rack. Two variances first: the primary says only 200-300K Rubin units in Q3 (the relay said 300-400K), and it names TWO causes for the ~two-month push-out — the thermal design change AND an HBM BASE-DIE issue — tying the slip to the same HBM4 rework SemiAnalysis described on 07-24. Then the half no relay carried: per Arthur's ODM checks the goal is still a fixed 144 GPUs per rack, but heat dissipation, cable design and existing datacenter power density are unsolved because GPU-to-rack connection is still cable-based, with a single rack potentially reaching ~1 MILLION WATTS — "Nvidia face a lot of problem, not only from upstream, it's from downstream." Hon Hai has put both two-rack and four-rack proposals to Nvidia, and that proposal process is itself the source of the recent market noise, not a leak about a decision. Timing: the Rubin rack starts shipping this December quarter, the new Rubin Ultra rack design lands ~December quarter 2027, clearer downstream info "at least before Q2 2027" — against Shang's own "we are already in August, so the timeline is very, very tight to enter production for Ruben Ultra." Net: the ⚠ stands and shifts venue — the silicon roadmap keeps being reaffirmed while the *rack* is still unfinished, and the constraint now named is thermal/power/cabling at the ODM, not packaging at the foundry. Read the "Titan" two-rack option (ASR: "Tycan"; logged on this page as "Taycan" from the 07-28/08-03 relays — same design) as still a proposal, not a plan. |

(1) These are MOUs, not deals** — the release states twice that the partnerships *"remain subject to execution of the final agreements"*, and this page's own precedent is that **NVDA and [[OPENAI]] signed an LOI last September for a "landmark strategic partnership" and abandoned it a few months later** (The Information · Peers, 07-27 row below). **(2) Three of the six are EXTENSIONS of existing NVDA relationships, not new counterparties — and the release's own quotes say so:** Fink cites the **AI Infrastructure Partnership**, Bae/Nuttall call NVDA *"a founding investor in Helix Digital Infrastructure"*, and **Brookfield** is already NVDA's partner in the **NAVER Korea national-AI-factory** expansion (07-24 row below). So the counterparty list is partly a formalisation of capital already inside the ecosystem, which is the check on reading $500bn as $500bn of *new* money. **(3) 🔴 THE RELEASE ITSELF HINTS NVDA PARTICIPATES IN THE ECONOMICS, WHICH IS WHERE THE FIRST-LOSS QUESTION ACTUALLY LIVES:** the subhead promises the platforms enable **"long-duration usage-linked revenue"** for NVIDIA — usage-linked revenue *is* the **07-02 revenue-share mechanic** (GPU backstop in exchange for a cut of cloud revenue), and you do not earn it without carrying some utilisation risk. **If NVDA takes no first loss, this is genuine de-circularisation and the dominant bear loses its core; if it does, it is the same circularity with third-party leverage stacked on top — worse, not better. That is the question for Kress/Hari on the 08-26 call.** ⚠️ **The "investable asset" claim maps 1:1 onto the three things credit needs, which is why it is written the way it is** — residual value (*"continuously improved through CUDA… extending its useful life"*), transferability (*"fungible and transferable across customers and operators"*), offtake (*"a deep global ecosystem… of offtakers"*) — **and this page's two standing bears attack exactly the first two:** Burry's depreciation-lever critique (07-09) and MSFT CFO Amy Hood's *"short-lived assets… if the demand environment changes, you just slow down"* (08-02). **The first platform's actual LTV and tenor will say more about year-5 GPU residual value than the $500bn headline does.** **Same-day, same structure:** [[ANTHROPIC]] + Macquarie + GIC formed **Theseus Infrastructure** for AI data centres — frontier lab and merchant-GPU vendor both moving compute off their own balance sheets into third-party infra vehicles in the same week (see [ANTHROPIC.md](ANTHROPIC.md)). (NVIDIA Newsroom, 2026-08-10; Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-10; sizing conta on the MS 07-27 $39bn/GW anchor — see Current state) |

The ENTIRE industry HBM demand cut (−4% / −10% / −19%) is NVIDIA; [[AMD]] is revised UP (+9% / +50% / +12%) and the ASIC block up 1% / 11% / 14%.** On procurement (t+4 months) the NVDA cut is −3% / −21% / −38%. **★ The consequence is the headline number for this page: HBM bit demand mix goes NVDA 69% ('25) → 59% ('26E) → 42% ('27E) → 45% ('28E) vs ASIC 22% → 35% → 48% → 46% — the custom-ASIC complex consumes MORE HBM bits than NVIDIA from 2027E on JPM's numbers.** ⚠️ **Read the cause honestly: roughly half that crossover is NVIDIA content coming DOWN, not ASIC units going up.** **The spec table behind it (JPMe):** *"Rubin Ultra — **4 compute dies originally to 2 compute dies**, and the lite version adopting **8-Hi HBM4E instead of 16-Hi/12-Hi HBM4E**"*; and *"the **Rubin GPU** (2 SKU variations with the lowest one adopting **192GB 12-Hi HBM4**)."* Content ladder in GB: **RU 16-Hi HBM4E 64GB × 16 = 1,024 → 12-Hi 48GB × 12 = 576**; **RU 12-Hi 48GB × 16 = 768 → 8-Hi 32GB × 12 = 384**; **Rubin 12-Hi 36GB × 8 = 288 → 8-Hi 24GB × 8 = 192**. **JPM's load-bearing assumption, stated explicitly and worth holding it to: "assume 70% of SKUs adopt the downsized HBM specs for Rubin/Rubin Ultra."** ⚠️ **That 70% is the number to attack if the MS tiered-SKU read (08-10, row below) is right that the mix is still open until end-3Q26 — JPM has effectively pre-committed to the bear side of a question MS says is unresolved.** JPM's own stated cause is supply, not demand: *"the primary reason is end-customers aiming to cope with the current shortage situation"* — and it flags the omission on the other side: *"our revision does NOT reflect the potential Rubin GPU demand UPSIDE from greater HBM supply availability, which remains an upside risk point."* **Supplier wallet share within NVDA: [[SKHYNIX]] 91% ('23) → 83% → 74% → 54% → 53% ('27E); [[SAMSUNG]] 9% → 9% → 1% → 24% → 24%; [[MU]] 0% → 8% → 25% → 22% → 22%** — Samsung's re-entry at ~24% is the notable move. **Also on NVIDIA from the storage side (Flash Memory Summit 2026): two named eSSD architectures — SCADA (GPU-direct-to-eSSD) and CMX (GPU→DPU→eSSD)** — *"aim to optimize latency/bandwidth by workload"*; and the **SOCAMM de-spec for Vera CPU (1.5TB → 768GB per CPU)** is confirmed from the memory side. | **08-09** | 🔴🔴 **SemiAnalysis — "Ultra-High Interactivity on NVIDIA GPUs? TileRT InferenceX." A SOFTWARE RESULT THAT DEFENDS NVIDIA'S WEAKEST FLANK — and quietly cannibalises Nvidia's own LPU line** | produto / competicao | **bull (with one sharp internal contradiction)** | **★ THE SETUP, WHICH IS THE HONEST STATEMENT OF THE GPU'S ONE STRUCTURAL WEAKNESS: "GPUs perform exceptionally well at HIGH THROUGHPUT AND LOW-TO-MEDIUM INTERACTIVITY, but their architecture is LESS SUITED FOR ULTRA-LOW-LATENCY inference… The gap comes from LATENCY RATHER THAN BANDWIDTH." An 8-GPU HGX B200 has 64 TB/s aggregate HBM bandwidth and GLM-5 at NVFP4 needs only ~21GB of active-parameter traffic per token, implying a roofline of 3,047 TOKENS/S/USER at batch 1 — "in practice, GPUs come NOWHERE CLOSE to this limit," because kernel launch/teardown and synchronisation dominate below ~1ms TPOT even with CUDA graphs.** 🔴 **AND THE LINE THAT BELONGS IN THE MEMORY DEBATE AS MUCH AS HERE: "although GPU MEMORY BANDWIDTH INCREASES BY ROUGHLY 2–3× EACH GENERATION, MEMORY LATENCY HAS NOT IMPROVED AT ALL… EVEN AS HBM PRICES CONTINUALLY INCREASE."** Cross-file [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). **THE RESULT: TileRT statically compiles the entire decode graph into ONE PERSISTENT KERNEL ("a CUDA graph optimizes the LAUNCHING of kernels; TileRT ABOLISHES THE KERNEL AS THE UNIT OF EXECUTION"), with warp- and whole-GPU specialisation (in GLM-5.1's attention layer "GPU 0 becomes a SPARSE INDEXER worker… GPUs 1 through 7 run the MLA workers"). On the InferenceX GLM-5.1 benchmark: at 8k/1k, TileRT hits 340 TOKENS/S/USER ON AN EIGHT-GPU B200 NODE vs a prior best of 181.4 on GB300 NVL72 with NVFP4+MTP (1.9×), and 3.0× the fastest FP8 result (113.6 on B300 with MTP). At 1k/1k, TileRT FP8 reaches 494.2 tok/s/user — 1.9× the best conventional FP4 (256.3) and 3.6× the best conventional FP8 (136.3) — "and TileRT DOESN'T YET HAVE FP4 SUPPORT." End-to-end latency 4.5× better at 1k/1k and 3.0× at 8k/1k, the decode tail being 3.01s vs 6.54s for the best NVFP4+MTP competitor and 18.18s for [[AMD]]'s MI355X.** ⚠️ **READ THE BASIS BEFORE READING THE HEADLINE: this is BATCH SIZE 1, one in-flight request per decode node (v0.1.5), and it is an interactivity result, NOT a throughput or cost-per-token result on its own. The trade is explicit — at 8k/1k the GB300 FP4+MTP point at concurrency 12 delivers ~240 total tok/s/GPU at 154 tok/s/user, while TileRT delivers 160.4 tok/s/GPU at 340 tok/s/user. "TileRT is not just a race car, but MORE LIKE A PRIVATE ROCKET SHIP WITH ROOM FOR JUST ONE PASSENGER."** ✅ **THE TCO NUMBERS, WHICH ARE WHAT MAKE IT A COMMERCIAL RESULT RATHER THAN A BENCHMARK: "At 8k/1k, TileRT reaches 340 tok/s/user while delivering 35.4 output tokens/s per B200. This comes out to $13.56 PER MILLION OUTPUT TOKENS." Against the best FP4 disagg point (GB200 FP4+MTP at concurrency 5, ~176 tok/s/user, $1.86/GB200-hour) at $13.4/M: "TileRT therefore costs ONLY 1% MORE PER TOKEN WHILE DELIVERING 1.9× THE INTERACTIVITY." Against the fastest conventional FP8 (GB300 FP8+MTP, 108 tok/s/user, ~$35/M): "61% CHEAPER PER OUTPUT TOKEN WHILE DELIVERING 3.1× THE INTERACTIVITY."** ⚠️ **The pricing benchmark they choose is a competitor's product tier: "this is MUCH MORE VALUE THAN CLAUDE CODE FAST MODE, UP TO 2.5× THE INTERACTIVITY BUT 2× THE PRICE PER TOKEN" — i.e. the premium fast-mode tiers labs are now selling are being repriced by open-source software running on installed GPUs. See [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).** 🔴 **THE STRATEGIC POINT, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS IS AN NVDA ROW: the fungibility argument. "A GPU POOL IS ONE LIQUID RESOURCE — excellent at prefill, excellent at high-to-medium-batch decode, and NOW SOMEWHAT CREDIBLY STRONG AT ULTRA-INTERACTIVE DECODE, with capacity moving between those roles as a SOFTWARE SCHEDULER DECISION that can follow demand hour by hour. AN ASIC FLEET IS THE OPPOSITE: the ratio of speed-tier capacity to everything else is FIXED IN HARDWARE THE DAY THE PURCHASE ORDER IS SIGNED… Guess wrong with GPUs and you REBALANCE IN SOFTWARE. Guess wrong with dedicated silicon and you either STRAND CAPITAL IN IDLE SPEED MACHINES or TURN AWAY THE EXACT PREMIUM TRAFFIC YOU BOUGHT THEM FOR." The market evidence given: TileRT decode is already in production behind Xiaomi's MiMo V2.5 Pro UltraSpeed and Z.ai's GLM-5.1 HighSpeed, and "NEITHER COMPANY PROCURED A NEW DATAFLOW CHIP. THEY CARVED A SPEED TIER OUT OF THE ACCELERATOR CLUSTER THEY ALREADY RAN… GOOD ENOUGH ON HARDWARE YOU ALREADY OWN TENDS TO BEAT ARCHITECTURALLY PURE ON HARDWARE YOU HAVE TO BUY."** ⚠️⚠️ **THE INTERNAL CONTRADICTION FOR NVIDIA SPECIFICALLY, AND NOBODY IN THE NOTE NAMES IT: the TAM this result attacks is not only [[CEREBRAS]]/Groq/SambaNova's — NVIDIA NOW SELLS INTO IT ITSELF. The note twice refers to "NVIDIA GROQ LPUs" and states that "the SemiAnalysis Accelerator Model has our quarter-by-quarter estimates of NVIDIA LPU30, LPU40, [[CEREBRAS]] WSE-3 & WSE-4 shipments." If TileRT lets a GB200/B200 fleet serve the speed tier at iso-cost, the first casualty inside Nvidia's own book is the LPU line it acquired Groq to own. Logged as an unresolved tension, not as a call.** ⚠️ **THE CEILING IS STILL PHYSICS, AND THE NOTE CONCEDES IT: "Cerebras serves a dense 70B at speeds NO EIGHT-GPU NODE CAN REACH regardless of scheduling: SOFTWARE CAN APPROACH THE HBM ROOFLINE, BUT IT CANNOT RAISE IT." Plus real adoption limits — model catalog is GLM-5/5.1 and DeepSeek-V3.2 only, "GLM5.1 IS A GENERATION BEHIND AND HAS ALREADY BEEN DEPRECATED ON MAINLINE INFERENCEX," hard-pinned driver stacks, and per-architecture compiler work ("change the attention mechanism or the routing scheme and MUCH OF THAT SCHEDULE IS INVALIDATED").** ✅ **DISCLOSURE ITEM WORTH DIARISING: "NVIDIA HAS COMMITTED TO SUBMITTING VERIFIABLE VERA RUBIN NUMBERS TO INFERENCEX. We will have [[GOOG]] TPUv7 results soon, and [[AMD]] has committed to MI455X UALoE72 THIS YEAR TOO."** ⚠️ **First-party Vera Rubin performance submitted to a third-party public benchmark is a checkable event and a break from the vendor-slide status quo — put it on the watch list.** (SemiAnalysis · Bryan Shan / Daniel Nishball / Cam Quilici / Kimbo Chen / Alec Ibarra / Dylan Patel, "Ultra-High Interactivity on NVIDIA GPUs? - TileRT InferenceX", published 2026-08-09; received via the daniel.grozdea research forward 2026-08-10) |
| **08-07** | 🔴 **UBS Memory Semis Monthly (Gaudois/Arcuri) — THE RUBIN ULTRA DE-SPEC IS SETTLED, AND IT SETTLES AGAINST THE 12-Hi CAMP. The single most decision-relevant row of this window.** | **supply / produto** | **mixed** | **★ THIS SUPERSEDES THE OPEN QUESTION LOGGED ON 08-06.** Yesterday this page recorded GFHK's "8-Hi design finalized, YET 12-Hi REMAINS ON LINE-UP" and flagged it as contested. UBS's supply-chain checks now come down on one side: **"Nvidia will likely INITIALLY USE HBM4 8-Hi FOR RUBIN ULTRA followed by A SWITCH TO HBM4E 8-Hi. This is compared to OUR PRIOR EXPECTATION OF RUBIN ULTRA USING HBM4E 12-Hi. WE BELIEVE THIS IS IN RESPONSE TO PERSISTENT TIGHT SUPPLY."** **Content math, explicit: the initial VR300 uses 384GB of HBM4 per GPU (8-Hi × 24Gb × 8 × 2), then moves to 512GB HBM4E (8-Hi × 32Gb × 8 × 2) — VERSUS UBS'S PRIOR 768GB ESTIMATE, i.e. a ~33% cut in per-GPU HBM content at the endpoint.** ⚠️ **This corroborates the SUPPLY reading of the de-spec (GFHK, 08-06) and refutes the demand reading — but note it also refutes GFHK's own claim that 12-Hi stays on the line-up. Both are supply-chain relays; UBS is the more specific and is dated later. DIGITIMES independently headlines the same direction the same day ("Nvidia tests LEANER RUBIN ULTRA MEMORY DESIGNS with 2027 HBM SUPPLY IN QUESTION", 2026-08-07).** 🔴 **AND HERE IS THE PART THAT MATTERS FOR NVDA RATHER THAN FOR MEMORY: UBS RAISED its 2027 NVDA HBM end-consumption to 25.1bn Gb (was 24.0bn) DESPITE the lower content per GPU — "as GPU UNITS IMPLIED GO UP TO 12.6MIL FROM 11.0MIL."** **De-spec plus MORE units is the bullish reading of a supply-constrained bill of materials: NVIDIA is trading memory per die for die count.** Industry context: total HBM end-consumption to **61.5bn Gb in 2027 (was 58.7bn), +91% y/y**, with **Google TPU HBM raised to 19.2bn Gb (was 17.4bn)** on unit upside; **HBM4E ASP ~US$3.9/Gb in '27 (+75% y/y), HBM4 US$3.5 (+60%), blended HBM ASP +79% y/y.** (UBS · Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri et al., 2026-08-07) |

Print date fixed: WED 2026-08-26 POST-CLOSE.** | guidance | **bull** | **The setup in numbers: BofA expects a $3-4bn beat at $94-95bn vs the $91bn guide, and a raise to $107-108bn vs sell-side ~$104bn — i.e. "NVDA's usual 3-4% beat/raise" — on the start of Vera Rubin shipments, new Vera CPU ramps and solid cloud capex. "ALL-TIME-HIGH SPOT GPU RENTAL PRICES point to upside on cloud pricing that could ADDRESS PERSISTENT ROI CONCERNS and drive faster next-gen adoption."** 🔴 **THE MEMORY-COST ANSWER IS THE NET-NEW ANALYTICAL CONTENT, AND IT IS SMALLER THAN THE NARRATIVE: "we see only a ~60BPS GM HEADWIND ON NVL COMPUTE RACKS in upcoming Vera Rubin (TOTAL DRAM ~12.7% OF RACK PRICE) vs current Blackwell Ultra (~12.1%)."** Long-term GM held at **~73-74% (vs ~75% today)**. ⚠️ **The caveat is where the dilution actually lives: "NEW POD-LEVEL FULL SYSTEMS COULD CARRY A ~500BPS GM DILUTION (due to LPDDR5X in CPU racks, NAND in storage racks), BUT WE EXPECT THEIR MIX TO BE SMALL vs traditional NVL racks."** ⚠️ **Note the tension with BofA's own framing elsewhere in the note that memory is "NOW 40-50% OF BUILD COST vs 15-20%+ HISTORICALLY" — the 40-50% is a BUILD-COST share, the 12.7% is a RACK-PRICE share. DIFFERENT DENOMINATORS; do not merge them.** Offsets cited: **preferential sourcing (LTAs, [[SKHYNIX]] ties) and dynamic pricing.** 🔴 **THE CIRCULAR-FINANCING BEAR IS QUANTIFIED AND DISMISSED: "direct equity investments into ecosystem partners (incl. $30BN [[OPENAI]], $10BN [[ANTHROPIC]]) total ~$70BN TO DATE, vs its ability to generate ~$470BN OF FCF over the next two years (CY26-27E). This is JUST 15% OF THE EXPECTED FCF, leaving ample room for its 50% FCF return pledge."** **And the separate ~$250BN OPENAI/SB ENERGY BACKSTOP is characterised: "NOT UPFRONT CAPITAL but a CONTINGENT, BACK-END-LOADED (2028-30+) GUARANTEE, TRIGGERED ONLY ON OPENAI DEFAULT — a common vendor-helped project finance structure."** **Valuation: "~16x fwd PE (CY27) or 0.3x PEG, THE LOWEST IN ~10 YEARS."** **The five named debates beyond the beat/raise: (1) Vera Rubin launch/supply update and any change to the $1Tn+ CY25-27 forecast; (2) OpenAI vendor-financing/backstop clarity and WHEN CASH CAN SHIFT TO BUYBACKS; (3) GM durability amid memory cost inflation; (4) hyperscaler vs ACIE growth; (5) open vs closed models and NVDA's SAM.** (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-07) |

That is a cost line on the vendor-financing / self-hosted-capacity leg of the business the page already tracks (the GPU-debt-backstop and 704MW HUT single-tenant items), not on merchant GPU margins.** **The scarcity mechanism, and it is intensifying: Governor Abbott has directed the PUCT and ERCOT to audit ALL data-center projects in ERCOT's interconnection process, and ERCOT has PAUSED the "Batch Zero" review that was due to publish classification results on 2026-08-07. The queue stands at 474 GW of REQUESTS with data centers ~90% of new requests** — ⚠️ **a queued/requested figure, NOT energised, NOT IT-load, NOT facility GW; not comparable to the GW figures elsewhere on this page (standing rule in `_meta/assumptions.md`).** Bernstein's conclusion: the audit **"throttles speculative data center pipeline and makes genuine sites with development history more valuable"** → **already-approved MW re-rate upward, and time-to-power lengthens for everyone building new.** ⚠️ **For NVDA that is two-sided: longer time-to-power is a revenue-timing risk on GPUs that need energised halls, while scarcer approved MW raises the value of the capacity NVDA has already contracted.** Miner names (WULF, CIFR, CORZ, CLSK, RIOT, IREN, MARA) are not wiki-covered. Eugene Investment & Securities: "NVIDIA appears to be discussing a plan to configure the MAIN version of Rubin Ultra with a 2-DIE structure, HBM4 8Hi, and 8 STACKS, and to add an HBM4E 8Hi version at a later point. If Rubin Ultra shifts from HBM4E 12Hi 384GB to HBM4 8Hi 192GB, NVIDIA'S 2027 HBM DEMAND WOULD BE REVISED DOWN BY ROUGHLY 10%, FROM 24.1BN Gb TO 21.6BN Gb"** (@jukan05, 2026-08-03). **TrendForce's framing: DRAM shortage persists through 2027, and with HBM4e 12-Hi qualification timelines uncertain NVDA has been re-evaluating Rubin Ultra's HBM configuration since Q3 2026 — now weighing HBM4e 8hi, HBM4 12hi and HBM4 8hi IN PARALLEL, final spec not set.** MS's desk read: **"the likely trade-off is LOWER HBM CAPACITY PER GPU IN EXCHANGE FOR MORE SHIPPABLE GPUs and reduced supply/cost exposure"** (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-04). 🔴 **THE KEY INTERPRETIVE POINT, and both the bulls and the analyst agree on it: this is a supply-side optimisation, not a demand signal. @jukan05: "the Rubin Ultra HBM despec is a SUPPLY ISSUE. NOT A DEMAND ISSUE… as much additional HBM will be produced as the supply headroom freed up by the shift from 12Hi to 8Hi." JPM's [[SKHYNIX]] note the same day: content adjustment is "OPTIMIZATION INITIATIVES OVER DEMAND CORRECTION (total HBM procurement demand is UNCHANGED)," with the 8Hi migration narrowing the supply-demand gap from 30-40% to 10-20% — i.e. STILL A SHORTAGE MARKET.** ⚠️ **The genuinely useful cross-check is that two large accelerator buyers gave OPPOSITE memory signals on 08-04: [[AMD]] said customers have "the opportunity to MODIFY MEMORY FOOTPRINT if TCO is not as significant in certain workloads," while [[SPCX]] said "memory is the MAIN LIMITING FACTOR… supply is increasing 20% annually, demand is spiking by ~200%." The reconciliation that fits both: HBM content per GPU is negotiable at the margin, aggregate bit demand still exceeds supply. Neither claim is promoted over the other.** ⚠️ **Note also the offsetting ODM check already on this page from 08-03 (Jefferies/Fubon: "NO DESPEC IN MEMORY FROM NVIDIA," procurement budgeting $7-8m per rack) — the primary supply-chain evidence and the Korean sell-side modelling still disagree.** Cross-filed [[SKHYNIX]], [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[AMD]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). |

| 08-03 | **Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update" — THE PRIMARY CALL TRANSCRIPT behind the two 08-03 relay rows below** | produto | **mixed** | ⚠️ **Machine transcript (ASR) — names are garbled throughout; corrected inline on first use.** This is the call itself, so it settles two variances in the relays and adds the whole DOWNSTREAM half of the story, which no relay carried. **(1) THE Q3 RUBIN NUMBER IS LOWER IN THE PRIMARY THAN IN THE RELAY — hold both, do not average: Shang says "we see only around 200, 300K Ruben units in the third quarter," vs the group-call summary's "300-400k in 3Q" logged below. Q4 700-800K is identical in both. He also flags these are TESTING volumes, "so there is a production lead time difference compared to CoWoS production volume"** — i.e. the CoWoS-model volume and the test-house volume are not the same quarter, which is exactly the seam through which the KYEC guide-down arrived. Push-out still implies **"better momentum in Q1 next year."** **(2) THE PUSH-OUT HAS TWO CAUSES, NOT ONE, AND THE SECOND ONE IS MEMORY: "due to the earlier, the thermal design change and the HBM based [base] die issue causing the production push out by around two months."** The page's 08-02 row carried only the thermal redesign; the **HBM base-die** co-cause and the **~two-month** magnitude are net-new — and they connect the Rubin slip directly to the HBM4 pin-speed rework SemiAnalysis described on 07-24 (Nvidia pushed HBM4 to 10.7 Gbps, forcing suppliers to rework the part). Two independent desks now attribute the slip to memory engineering, not demand. 🔴 **(3) THE HBM4 $/GB YEAR IS AMBIGUOUS IN THE PRIMARY SOURCE AND MUST BE RECORDED AS SUCH: in prepared remarks Shang says HBM4 cost rises "from 17 to $18 per gigabyte to 30 to 32 per gigabyte IN 2026"; in the Q&A (Alvin) the same analyst on the same call says "the HBM cost IN 2027 will increase to around 30 to 32 per gigabyte." Same $30-32 range, two different years, one speaker. Do NOT pick one — this page already carries $31-32/GB (deck) and O'Mara's relay "$30-31 per GB NEXT YEAR."** ⚠️ **Why the year matters more than the dollar: if the step is a 2026 event, the Rubin ASP/gross-margin arithmetic and the memory-cost hit to the rack BOM land a full year earlier than the page's 2027 framing assumes.** **(4) AND THE $78-80K RUBIN ASP IS A DERIVED ESTIMATE, NOT A DISCLOSED PRICE — verbatim: "assuming Nvidia can maintain a gross margin of 75 to 80% on the chip, we estimate that Ruben's GPU price will skyrocket to 78 to 80,000 per chip." The GM is the INPUT. So the $78-80k cannot be cited as evidence that NVDA holds mid-to-high-70s GM through the HBM step-up — that is circular; it is only a statement of what price would be required.** **(5) RUBIN ULTRA HBM CONTENT — the arithmetic behind the relay's "unchanged," from an independent supply chain: the CANCELLED four-compute-die Rubin Ultra was heard at ">1,024 gigabytes per chip"; the SURVIVING two-compute-die version at "around 384 gigabytes" (Shang hedges: "I'm not sure this is the real number, latest number"). Blackwell reference points from the same Q&A: B200 ~192 GB, B300 ~288 GB, on HBM3-class at $17-18/GB. So the correct reading is: NVDA has not changed HBM content per configuration — "we haven't seen Nvidia changing the HBM content in Ruben Ultra" — but the configuration that survived is the lower-content one. This independently corroborates Arete's 07-21 "Rubin Ultra reverts to 384GB HBM4E from 1TB" from a supply-chain rather than a research angle.** Memory-supplier detail on [[MU]] / [[SKHYNIX]] / [[SAMSUNG]] and [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). **(6) TIMING, from Arthur's ODM checks: the Rubin rack "will be start to shipping on the this year December quarter," and the NEW Rubin Ultra rack design "probably will be December quarter of 2027," with clearer downstream information "at least before the second quarter of 2027." Against Shang's own warning — "we are already in August, so the timeline is very, very tight to enter production for Ruben Ultra."** 🔴 **(7) THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS DOWNSTREAM, NOT UPSTREAM — the freshest thing on the call and absent from every relay. The fixed goal is unchanged at 144 GPUs per rack ("that's for sure"); what is not solved is heat dissipation, cable design, and existing datacenter power density, because GPU-to-rack connection is STILL CABLE-BASED, with a single rack potentially reaching ~1 MILLION WATTS. Verbatim: "Nvidia face a lot of problem, not only, I think it's not from upstream, it's from downstream." Nvidia is working with HON HAI, which has put multiple rack proposals (both two-rack and four-rack) to Nvidia — "so this is why a lot of noisy recently," i.e. the ODM proposal process IS the market noise, not a leak about a decision.** Cross-check against the page's own power ladder (JPM: VR Ultra ~1.5MW rack, 800V HVDC) — Arthur's ~1MW is the ODM-side view of the same wall, and it is being described as a reason the design is not finished rather than a spec. **(8) RACK BOM, both ends of the comparison: "the GP 200 [GB200] per rack is running 3 million or 3.5 million. For Rubin, there will be 7 million to 8 million"** — driven by HBM price inflation. The $7-8M was already on the page from the O'Mara relay; the **~$3-3.5M GB200-class base**, and therefore the **~2.3x rack-BOM step**, are net-new. And the ODMs' response is a capital one: **"for their purchasing team they say they are rising the capex for buy and sell for the GPU"** — ODMs raising capex specifically to fund buy-and-sell of GPUs for 2027, which is a working-capital tell that they believe the volumes. **(9) THE MEMORY-DESPEC REBUTTAL, WIDENED BEYOND NVDA: ODMs told Arthur that downsizing the memory module was discussed but "there will be very serious impact the AI server performance" and is unfeasible; and looking at 2027 specs, Hon Hai and "Weichuang" [ASR-garbled Taiwanese ODM] "don't see any spec change for 2027 — including the Google [[GOOG]], AWS [[AMZN]]." So the no-despec check is not an NVDA-only datapoint; it covers the two largest custom-ASIC programs too.** Named supply-chain casualty **KYEC (ASR: "KYC")** — 2026 revenue guide cut from "up low-40s%" to "high-30s%", with Fubon expecting others to follow — is logged by the outros-asia agent; KYEC has no page here. Cross-file [[TSM]] (CoWoS capacity), [[INTC]] (EMIB), [[MEDIATEK]], [[AVGO]], [[AMD]], [[GOOG]], [[AMZN]]. (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_sherman_update_03_08_26.html) |

Increasing MTK Allocations & HBM Cost Assumptions"** | produto | **mixed** | 🔴 **The live Q&A version of the 07-28 Fubon primary note already on this page — most numbers reconfirm, but FIVE things are net-new and they matter. (1) THE PRIOR RUBIN NUMBER IS NOW NAMED: "Rubin — we now est FY26 shipment to revised down to 1mn from prev 1.5mn." The page carried the 1mn mark from the 08-02 relay without the base; the cut is ONE-THIRD, not a trim.** **(2) THE SUBSTITUTION IS QUANTIFIED QUARTER BY QUARTER, and it is the reason NVDA itself is unharmed: Rubin testing volumes imply "300-400k in 3Q, 700-800k in 4Q," with "better momentum in 1Q"; Blackwell did 1.7mn in 2Q and is "likely FLAT q/q in 3Q at 1.6-1.7mn," declining in 4Q only once Rubin ramps. Verbatim on the netting: "NET NET NEUTRAL IMPACT TO NVDA, but some -VE IMPACT TO SUPPLY CHAIN."** ⚠️ **(3) THE SUPPLY-CHAIN CASUALTY IS NAMED AND HAS ALREADY GUIDED: "KYEC guided down FY revs — from low-40's to high-30's [% y/y growth]. That is the first confirmed earnings-level damage from the Rubin slip, and Fubon says it is not the last — treat any Rubin-spec-upgrade beneficiary's 2H guide as at risk.** **(4) THE RUBIN HBM SUPPLY SEQUENCE IS SPELLED OUT, AND HYNIX IS THE LATE ONE: "only see SMALL VOLUME of [[MU]] and [[SAMSUNG]] HBM in supply chain"; "Rubin with [[SKHYNIX]]'s HBM4 will only start ramping in LATE-AUG/SEP with BIGGER VOLUME." Same story, ±$1.** **(5) RUBIN ULTRA HBM CONTENT IS UNCHANGED despite the redesign — "have not seen any change in Rubin Ultra HBM content": the 4-compute-die design was cancelled and the 2-compute-die version carries less HBM per die, but the rack-level content holds.** Reconfirmed without change: total NVDA chip output **8.2mn 2026 → 12.4mn 2027**; NVDA ≈**50% of TSMC CoWoS allocation, 630k wafers 2026 → >1mn 2027** (TSMC + ASE/SPIL combined); **HBM4 at $31-32/GB vs $17-18/GB for HBM3e**, implying a **Rubin GPU price of $78-80k** at a held 75-80% chip GM; the **"Taycan" two-rack alternative (144 GPUs × 2 compute dies, NPO/CPO for scale-out between racks, cable for scale-up inside)** vs four bundled Oberon racks — **"design not yet completed… but we are already in Aug, so the timeline is VERY TIGHT to enter Rubin Ultra production."** On optics the call is unambiguous: **"NVDA still very aggressive on NPO/CPO solutions."** Slide-deck variance worth noting: Beavington's deck puts **end-2027 TSMC CoWoS at 188k wpm** against the primary note's 180k — treat 180k as the published figure. (a) The mechanism is ENGINEERING, not demand — a thermal redesign forcing a production push-out — which is consistent with the pattern this page already carries from SemiAnalysis (Kyber NVL144 delayed 12+ months to 2028 on the PCB midplane, NVL576 delayed, NVL72x2 cancelled, 2026-07-06). ⚠️ Two independent sources now describe Nvidia's next-generation rack-scale roadmap slipping for physical-integration reasons rather than silicon reasons. That is the most persistent negative on this page and it is being corroborated, not refuted.** **(b) The revenue offset is real but NOT margin-neutral: substituting Blackwell for Rubin holds unit volume and TSMC wafer demand roughly constant (see [[TSM]]) while pushing the ASP/content step-out to the right. Anyone modelling a 2026 ASP uplift off the Rubin transition should mark it down.** ⚠️ **Fubon's SoIC capacity cut in the same note (TSMC 3D-stacking to 70k wpm by Q4-28 from 90k, CoWoS held at 220k with upside) is the packaging-side fingerprint of the same story — the marginal AI die stays CoWoS-attached rather than SoIC-stacked over the forecast window.** |
| 08-02 | **Culpium · Tim Culpan — "[[MSFT]] Just Said the Nvidia Part Out Loud"** | demanda | **bear** | ⚠️ **THE DEMAND-SIDE RISK STATED BY THE BUYER, AND IT IS THE MOST DIRECT BEAR ARGUMENT ON THIS PAGE THIS QUARTER. Microsoft CFO Amy Hood, asked how Microsoft protects itself against data-centre or chip overbuild: "You've seen our CapEx really pivot toward what I would call… SHORT-LIVED ASSETS, which really, that's CPUs and GPUs that have relatively shorter lead times… IF THE DEMAND ENVIRONMENT CHANGES, YOU JUST SLOW DOWN WHAT IS, IN FACT, THE LARGEST COMPONENT AND THE DRIVER OF COGS," and "WHEN YOU HAVE THE ABILITY TO LATE BIND SOME OF THE MORE EXPENSIVE COMPONENTS IN SHORT TERM, YOU HAVE A BIG BOOK OF BUSINESS THAT'S FLEXIBLE."** **Culpan's read: the largest buyer of Nvidia silicon has publicly described the option to slow-walk GPU purchases while keeping headline capex and shell construction intact — "a game of capacity chicken" in which "executives are incentivized to publicly maintain their long-term capex targets while fiddling with the actual amount of compute they install quarter-to-quarter." He argues the structure is specifically bad for Nvidia and [[AMD]] "because these companies BOOK CAPACITY YEARS IN ADVANCE and face VERY SHORT PRODUCT LIFE CYCLES. Inventory on the shelf is poison in the veins," and cites [[SPCX]] and [[META]] both starting to offload their own compute to third parties as evidence someone over-built.** ⚠️ **[[AMZN]]'s Jassy said the same thing in his own words on the same earnings week: "we typically purchase these A FEW MONTHS BEFORE putting them into service, so we have strong visibility into customer demand before we trigger the spend. IF THE DEMAND ISN'T THERE, WE WON'T SPEND THE CAPITAL." And [[META]]'s CFO left 2028 "wide open," framed as securing land and power while DEFERRING CHIPS. Three of the four largest customers have now described the same late-binding option inside one fortnight. Whether it is exercised is the question; that it exists and is being articulated to investors is new.** **The counterweight, and it is not weak: all four hyperscalers RAISED 2026 capex this season (aggregate ~$860bn CY26 → ~$1.2tn CY27 per BofA/Arya, 2026-08-02, with ~$2.3tn of customer commitments behind it), Azure guided to +45% on capacity that "continues to exceed" supply, and MS's supply-chain team reads the raises as hyperscalers "raising spending to ABSORB [component] costs RATHER THAN CUTTING UNIT VOLUMES" (MS · Howard Kao, 2026-08-02). The arbitrating observable is the SHELL-BUILD vs GPU-INSTALL divergence over the next 2-3 quarters — i.e. watch capex MIX (short- vs long-lived assets), which Microsoft now discloses, rather than headline capex, which has become a deliberately uninformative number.** |
| 08-02 | **AlphaSense expert call — Senior Manager at NSCALE (GPU-as-a-service operator)** | supply | **mixed** | **A neocloud operator's view of what actually gates deployment, and it is not silicon. The expert — a government/regulatory-affairs and energy-policy specialist who secures power for large-scale DC projects — is "OPTIMISTIC about the continued demand for large-scale data centers and the ability to secure necessary power through innovative solutions like BEHIND-THE-METER NATURAL GAS GENERATION," with the organisation partnering with major hyperscalers to provide GPU-as-a-service on sites "powered PRIMARILY BY NATURAL GAS due to current energy constraints."** ⚠️ **THE BOTTLENECK IS NAMED AND IT IS MECHANICAL: "Securing land, power, and ESPECIALLY LARGE QUANTITIES OF NATURAL GAS-FIRED ENGINES are significant challenges, with SUPPLY CHAIN CONSTRAINTS BEING A MAJOR BOTTLENECK." That is a direct corroboration of the reciprocating-engine/turbine scarcity thesis on [[BE]], [[GEV]] and [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md) from a buyer of the equipment rather than a seller of it.** Other content: land ACQUISITION preferred over leasing for risk mitigation; **Europe "more progressive on renewable energy requirements, with easier access to HYDROPOWER in regions like Scandinavia, while the U.S. relies more on natural gas and faces STRICTER SUPPLY LIMITATIONS"**; expansion potential into Australia; and the customer-mix call — **"the market will evolve to include MORE DIVERSE CUSTOMERS BEYOND HYPERSCALERS, including regular corporations and government entities,"** with capacity shifting **closer to population centres** over time. On policy: **"cautiously positive about the regulatory and political environment in the U.S., noting STRONG CURRENT GOVERNMENT SUPPORT but acknowledging POTENTIAL RISKS WITH FUTURE POLICY CHANGES."** ⚠️ **That last hedge now has a specific instance: four US states rolled back or paused data-centre sales-tax incentives this summer with nine more considering repeal, and Texas's governor has directed the legislature to "repeal sales tax exemptions… for data centers" when it reconvenes in 2027 — on a ~$40bn IT-equipment bill per gigawatt, a 7% sales tax is ~$3bn/GW of incremental cost, i.e. "7% or more to equipment costs" (The Information · Ann Davis Vaughan, 2026-08-02). It raises the customer's cost of Nvidia silicon without touching Nvidia's price.** 2 pages, consultant tier, no numbers disclosed. |

| 07-28 | **Fubon Research · Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang — "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates" (the PRIMARY note; distributed by Jefferies)** | supply | mixed | 🔴 **This is the source PDF behind the two Jefferies sales relays below, and it settles both numbers those relays disagreed on.** **CORRECTION 1 — the interposer counts are inverted in the Wong relay.** Fubon verbatim: *"We currently estimate **12 and 10 interposers per CoWoS wafer for Rubin and Rubin Ultra, respectively**"* — i.e. **Rubin 12, Rubin Ultra 10**, not the other way round. The conclusion is unaffected: after the Kyber / four-compute-die Rubin Ultra cancellation, Fubon now models Rubin Ultra's interposer as only **marginally larger** than Rubin's, which is what lifts **total NVDA chip output to 12.4mn in 2027 vs 8.2mn in 2026**. **CORRECTION 2 — the SoIC direction, resolved.** Fubon verbatim: *"Compared to our earlier forecast of **90k** capacity in 4Q28, we revise down our capacity expansion forecast to **70k in 4Q28**."* So **70k is the new mark and 90k the superseded one** — Beavington's relay was right, Wong's inverted. Cause: **[[TSM]] is slowing SoIC expansion to prioritise CoWoS**, which is also why Fubon sees **potential upside to its 220kwpm end-2028 CoWoS number** (end-2027 held at 180kwpm). **NET-NEW, and the number to carry: NVDA's TOTAL capacity allocation reaches 1.03mn wafers in 2027 vs 629k in 2026.** ⚠️ **Do not conflate this with the TSMC-only CoWoS line** — Fubon's Figure 5 has NVDA at **50.1% (2026) → 50.0% (2027)** of TSMC CoWoS = **586,500 → 945,000 wafers** (346,800 in 2025 at 51.4%). The gap between 945k and 1.03mn is **SPIL capacity supporting Vera CPU production**, which sits outside the TSMC CoWoS pool. **NET-NEW BOM, and it is a relative-advantage datapoint the page did not have: HBM4 costs NVDA US$31-32/GB, up from US$17-18/GB for HBM3e in 2026 — but "for other GPU and custom-ASIC projects, we believe their HBM cost will be higher than nVidia's and may reach US$35-36 per GB, meaning the HBM cost will double in 2027."** So HBM inflation is real (~+80% for NVDA) but **NVDA buys it ~10-15% cheaper than the merchant/ASIC field** — a scale edge that partly offsets the ASIC-TCO bear. On that BOM, Fubon models the **Rubin GPU price "skyrocketing" to US$78-80K assuming NVDA holds 75-80% chip gross margin** (confirmed verbatim against the Beavington relay). **NET-NEW RACK ARCHITECTURE — the most detailed account on this page of what replaces Kyber.** Kyber was cancelled/delayed **in March/April on mid-plane bandwidth issues**; it carried **576 compute dies per rack (144 GPUs × 4 compute dies)**, and Fubon's checks say **NVDA still plans the same 576 compute dies in the replacement rack or system**. Two candidate designs are in play: **(a) the conservative option — stay on the current Oberon rack, with one system built from four racks connected by traditional scale-out; each Oberon rack holds 144 compute dies, so 4 × 144 = 576**; **(b) a two-rack design Fubon calls "Taycan" — each rack has 144 GPUs × 2 compute dies, with NPO/CPO used for the scale-out connection BETWEEN the two racks and cable retained for scale-up inside each rack.** **Neither is finalised, and Fubon is explicit that it is therefore "difficult to say who will benefit and who will suffer" — it declines to name winners and losers. The one directional call it does make: "we think nVidia is still aggressive in the NPO/CPO solution despite a lot of market noise."** **Positioning, stated plainly and it is the bear side of this note: "in the short term, we think nVidia's supply chain could potentially underperform due to Rubin's lower-than-expected volume in 2H26 and uncertain design change in the next generation rack design,"** with Fubon more confident in the **CPU supply chain, the SPE supply chain and TPU momentum**. **Offsetting, on the demand debate: "despite the market's AI inflation concern recently, in our view, the token cost is still a more important factor in driving CSP's capex, and we remain positive about AI market demand."** Sector rating **Overweight**. (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html) |
| 07-27 | **Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak / Stephen Byrd / Adam Wood — "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC" (Internet · North America)** | demanda | **bull** | **A demand-durability read-through, not an NVDA call — the note carries no NVDA rating or PT. What makes it worth logging here is that MS standardises all three of its GenAI unit-economics frameworks on 1GW of NVDA GB300**, i.e. the buy-side argument for continued NVDA demand is now being made in the currency of NVDA's own installed base. **The shared base case: 410,256 GB300 chips per GW; all-in capital cost of US$39bn/GW** (US$23bn IT on a 5-yr life + US$16bn non-IT on a 15-yr life, ≈US$6bn/yr of depreciation for owned infrastructure), **assuming ~US$4mn per GB300 rack** ("consistent with our Asia semis' teams published rack BoM"); **75% utilisation**; **US$8.50/hr GPU rental**; **2,750 tokens/sec/GPU**. **The outputs: hyperscaler GPU rental (IaaS) generates ~US$22.9bn revenue/GW, ~67% incremental EBIT margin and ~31% ROIC** (scenario range 23%-39% ROIC across US$7.00-10.00/hr); **a model-enabled API on OWNED infrastructure earns ~46% ROIC / ~75% incremental margin**; **the same API on 3P/rented infrastructure earns ~25% ROIC / ~31% margin.** Read for this page: the ROIC math on the *current* generation clears a normal corporate hurdle at every layer of the stack, which is the direct counter to the Crisafulli / Chanos / Burry "hyperscalers can't generate FCF" bear already on the page — and it is a cleaner rebuttal than the token-cost/Jevons arguments because it is a capital-returns calculation on hardware already deployed. ⚠️ Note MS's own caveat, carried rather than smoothed: "**while custom ASICs and prior GPU generation have lower costs, we believe chip pricing/hour and token throughput are also lower**" — the framework is not a claim that GB300 wins on TCO, only that its economics work. (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html) |

| 07-29 | Aurelion Research/PhotonCap · Lumentum mgmt meeting (read-through) | produto | mixed | **Read-through only — NVDA is the customer, not the subject. Source is a joint independent publication: management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research (met Lumentum VP-IR Kathryn Ta), technical comments by PhotonCap; answers are the authors' paraphrase.** **(1) Why NVDA is deploying CPO now, per Lumentum management: today's co-packaged optics is "opportunistic rather than forced. Nvidia deploys it because a faster, more power efficient laser frees up power that can be redirected to the GPUs, which buys system performance. Copper has not run out of headroom yet."** She dates **copper hitting the wall in late 2027**, when the first scale-up CPO deployments (or MPO fiber connectors) arrive, driven by rack-to-rack link lengths beyond ~3m (Lumentum mgmt via Aurelion Research, 2026-07-29). **(2) The power arithmetic, with an NVDA-sourced number: "a data center runs against a fixed power envelope, so every watt saved on networking is a watt handed to the GPUs, and Nvidia's own material puts CPO at 3.5x lower power than pluggable transceivers. Copper is not forcing this wave; the power budget is."** ⚠️ **PhotonCap's caveat is the one to keep: "the weak spot is not the voluntariness but that it rides on one company's architecture choice"** — i.e. the whole optical complex is levered to NVDA's rack decision, which is exactly the unresolved Fubon post-Kyber question already on this page (PhotonCap technical comment, 2026-07-29). **(3) Two Rubin/domain mechanics, net-new to this page: "Rubin relies on bi-directional signaling to reach its bandwidth targets, and bi-directional signaling over copper degrades substantially at 448G per lane," with an unknown time-to-market for 448G-per-lane copper while "optics can scale along modulation, fiber count, and wavelength multiplexing"; and "Nvidia has begun moving from 72 GPU domains toward as many as 576 by 2027, and copper is already confined on an NV72 rack" — once a domain spans multiple racks, "linking must be optical regardless of copper's current efficiency."** Aurelion's conclusion is the framing worth stealing: **the optics thesis "necessitates that AI systems continue scaling, not only that copper hits a wall"** — so CPO scaling in FY27/FY28 "can do so in parallel with copper and still see commercial success" (Aurelion Research, 2026-07-29). **(4) Supplier posture: PhotonCap notes "NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent," and that "the same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B" — i.e. NVDA is actively second-sourcing its external light source, not locking one vendor.** Jensen's own framing is cited as **copper for scale-up, photonics for scale-out**, with NVDA saying there is a need for copper capacity even while investing in both LITE and COHR. See [LITE.md](LITE.md), [COHR.md](COHR.md), [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md). |
| 07-29 | SemiAnalysis · "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters" | produto | bull | **NVDA has extended the reference design from the rack to the building, and the vendor ecosystem is now designing to it — the clearest evidence yet that NVDA sets the FACILITY spec, not just the compute spec.** **DSX** — the reference design for the entire AI factory — was **first unveiled as an Omniverse digital-twin blueprint at GTC Washington in October 2025, formalized as the Vera Rubin DSX reference design in March 2026, and since expanded into the full DSX platform.** Coverage now spans compute, networking, storage and hardware cluster design **plus the facilities side — power, cooling, controls, even civil, structural and architectural design** (SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters", 2026-07-29). **Two named products worth tracking as separate businesses: "Nvidia's DSX Max-Q maximizes token per watt within a fixed power budget, and DSX Flex facilitates the connection [of] the facility to grid services, dynamically adjusting power draw and orchestrating demand with hybrid onsite generation"** — i.e. **DSX Flex pulls demand-response / behind-the-meter orchestration into the reference architecture itself**, a capability that had been the utility's or the operator's problem (see [themes/ai-datacenter-power.md](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md), [themes/800v-dc-power.md](themes/800v-dc-power.md)). **Workflow:** via the Omniverse DSX Blueprint an operator builds a digital twin, simulates layouts, power topologies, thermal behaviour and operational policies in real time, optimizes **before construction begins**, then reuses the same validated architecture across sites — **CoreWeave is already using DSX Air to build and test digital twins of its AI factories** (see [[CRWV]]). **The ecosystem adopting it is the datapoint:** Cadence, Dassault Systèmes, Eaton, Jacobs, Nscale, Phaidra, Procore, PTC, Schneider Electric, Siemens, Switch, Trane, Vertiv — with **Vertiv's OneCore now the building block of the Vera Rubin DSX reference design as of March 2026** (standardized 12.5 MW pods), **Schneider on 30+ NVIDIA reference designs** (EcoStruxure Pod, up to 40 racks) and pushed upstream into validated Vera Rubin facility designs, **Siemens among the first suppliers with both IEC and UL variants for Rubin DSX**, **Jacobs one of only TWO engineering firms working with the DSX reference architecture (the other Bechtel)**, **Hut 8's Beacon Point designed around DSX**, and **Foxconn unveiling its Modular Data Center concept at GTC 2026** with Schneider. Read: this is moat-widening beyond CUDA — it standardizes the *building* around NVDA's roadmap, raising the switching cost of a competing accelerator to include the facility, and it lands squarely on the page's open question of whether the optical/power complex is levered to "one company's architecture choice" (the PhotonCap caveat in the 07-29 Lumentum row directly above). ⚠️ Note the flip side: a reference architecture that reaches into civil/structural design also means **NVDA now owns more of the schedule risk** — if DSX facility specs churn, the whole vendor base re-tools. See [[VRT]], [[ETN]], [[FLEX]], [[PWR]], [[NVT]]. |

**Quarter synthesis:** flow **split** — demand/product bulls (Moore Top Pick PT $288, Arya PO $320, Barclays OW $275, Cantor 40 GW) against a growing chorus on **margin/competition** (Redburn HBM +70–80%, JPM ASIC>GPU in '27, Chanos/Crisafulli on sustainability). On Jun-24 two events added pressure to the bear side: (1) **Jalapeño (OpenAI+Broadcom)** — the world's largest lab announced its own inference chip; (2) **GB300 NVL72 firmware bug** (SemiAnalysis): racks need a reboot every 66.5 days. New from 06-27: (3) **A100 spot +20%** — installed base still price-appreciating despite Blackwell ramp, demand is broad; (4) **SambaNova $10B** — inference-ASIC ecosystem gaining VC conviction (mixed for NVDA's inference share); (5) **AI accelerators 11x 2022–26** — structural confirmation of the demand decade thesis. Near-term: hardware-to-software rotation narrative is creating intraday flow pressure but no fundamental shift. Net: demand thesis intact in training, debate intensified on inference share, margin (HBM) and firmware execution. **06-29/30:** Citi makes DRAM/HBM allocation the new ranking metric and puts NVDA #1 (HBM as advantage, not just consignment risk); SemiAnalysis's Accelerator Model sees 2H FY27 DC compute ~20% above consensus with Rubin's ramp back on track post-HBM4 fix; expert calls stay constructive on NVDA's position but flag the CUDA moat eroding (open-source inference engines) and physical-AI/humanoid as a real-but-back-end-loaded TAM. **06-30/07-01:** unit story turns more bullish into 2027 — UBS (Sunny Lin) raises Vera CPU units to 5.5mn in 2027 (from 1.6mn 2026, +57% CoWoS demand) and keeps Rubin at 2.1mn units for 2026 despite a slower Q2 ramp on redesigns; Jefferies/Favuzza (citing SemiAnalysis) sees 2H FY27 DC compute ~20% above consensus on the same post-HBM4-fix Rubin ramp; JPM's Data Center Watch shows GPU rental pricing still rising across all tiers in June and OpenRouter token volume/spend up 70% m/m, a live demand-pull signal; MS (Moore) prefers NVDA on tightening supply/demand even as broader semi inventory sits 33 days above median; the xAI→Anthropic Colossus 1 resale ($1.25bn/month, 220k+ GPUs) reinforces that next-gen compute stays scarce even as older-gen capacity recycles; two AlphaSense expert calls corroborate the moat debate — NVIDIA still dominant today but competitors catching up (ex-AMD manager), and a strong training moat vs rising inference competition from Trainium/AMD/Cerebras (ex-Cerebras/AWS advisor). **07-06 (MS TMT Sales / MS Semis Weekly / SemiAnalysis):** the flow turns two-sided. Bear-ish for the scale-up roadmap — SemiAnalysis (via CNBC & MS TMT) reports the **Kyber NVL144 rack delayed 12+ months to 2028** on PCB-midplane manufacturability, **NVL576 delayed/small-volume**, the **NVL72x2 alternative cancelled**, and **CPO NVSwitch not available until Feynman**, leaving NVDA with no proven way to grow the scale-up world size for **Rubin Ultra** and opening a window for **AMD MI500X** and **GOOGL TPUv8i 'Broadfly'** (an honest competitive-risk nuance, not spun). Offsetting on the demand/capital side: MS/Joe Moore reads the GPU shortage as still binding ("excess demand, not excess supply" — subletting a positive for NVDA share), and SemiAnalysis frames the **>$7T-by-2029 AI-debt market with NVDA as the "central bank"** revenue-guaranteeing neoclouds — bull for buildout continuity, but deepening the circular-financing critique. Net: demand/financing thesis intact; the sharpest new bear is the **scale-up-world-size gap for Rubin Ultra** while CPO/Kyber slip. **07-10 (MS NDR):** Joe Moore's primary post-NDR note reiterates NVDA as MS's Top Pick in semis (PT $288, bull $330/bear $160) and reframes the story around a **new 3-segment growth framework** (AI labs ~20% / traditional hyperscalers ~half / AI-clouds-industrial-enterprise ~half of DC rev), with **both NVDA & AVGO growing 80%+ next year and supply-constrained** (management/MS argue no dramatic ASIC share shift; lowest cost/token frequently NVDA), the **roadmap on track** (Rubin Ultra ships next year — NOT 2028; Kyber replaced by a "better solution"; 800V/optical on schedule — a 3rd corroboration of NVDA's 07-07 denial + Citi's 07-08 IR check), a multi-year memory shortage forcing content trade-offs (LPDDR5/rack halved), and — the newest angle — **50%+ of cash flow now moving to cash return**, positioning NVDA as a "value name" and broadening the NDR to value investors. Reinforces the demand/product/capital-return bull into 2Q; the 07-10 desk flow (UBS Arcuri Buy $280 on a soft-but-normalizing Taiwan-ADP proxy; Jefferies/Curtis, MS TMT/Wigg, Citi/Terry all constructive on the META/SPCX frontier-release read-through) rounds out a bullish close to the intra-quarter window. **07-17:** Jefferies (with Fubon, post-TSMC 2Q) adds a supply-side demand tell — NVDA is **increasing its Blackwell wafer orders at TSMC** into a still-very-tight wafer environment (TSMC "super-hot" rush runs still heavily used, GM-accretive for TSMC), with flagship GPUs on **CoWoS-L** and some products' **CoWoS-S/R** increasingly pushed to OSATs (ASE/Amkor) on TSMC capacity constraint — reinforcing the persistent-shortage / incremental-demand read, no house estimate changed. **07-20 (memory-selloff / thematic cluster):** MS (Joe Moore, "US memory selloff = entry point") calls NVDA + AVGO **"the best value in the market"** at ~22x CY27 EPS $13.08, preferring the compute names over memory on risk-reward; MS (Stephen Byrd, "Bitcoin-to-DC") adds a thematic compute-supply anchor (~140%/yr CAGR in NVDA compute sold; DC token-sale net margins Blackwell ~60% / Rubin ~80% / Feynman ~90%); Citrini's on-prem/sovereign-AI reference-architecture piece widens the marginal buyer to enterprise/sovereign/neocloud (bull); against it, Octahedron's 2Q26 LP call (FUND estimate) flags NVDA's **"compute-now-pay-later"** vendor financing as a demand-durability **"yellow flag"** — small/trial for now, core demand intact. Also folded the month-old **Bernstein "CPU Renaissance" note (06-17, Outperform PT $315)** — the first Bernstein rating on the page — and a **stale Q1'26 AlphaSense ex-AWS read-through** (Trainium ~25-35% cheaper, ~18-mo NVDA design cadence) as vintage-flagged historical context in Debate. **07-22/23:** BofA/Arya (reit Buy, PO $350) frames the Vera-vs-AMD **"faster agents vs more agents"** CPU debate on NVDA's most detailed Vera disclosure yet (88 Olympus cores, 1.2TB/s mem BW, 3.4TB/s on-die fabric, monolithic die, $170bn 2030E server-CPU TAM) into AMD's 07-23 AI Day; @jukan05 adds a NAND read-through (Rubin CMX = 576 SSDs / 9,600TB; industry CMX NAND demand 35M→>100M TB y/y) and a manufacturability nuance (Rubin Ultra 5.5x-reticle implementation difficulties, 9.5x "extremely difficult" on soldering — cf. Arete's 07-21 two-die re-scoping); NVDA disclosed ~9.3% of Nebius (capital/bull); Redburn stays Buy ("Why the Vera-Rubin Media Offensive?"). No house/BBG estimate superseded. **07-24 (SemiAnalysis dedup + backfill pass):** two additions of substance. (1) The **AMD Advancing-AI teardown** ("Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 07-24) is the sharpest competitive scorecard the page carries — it upgrades AMD's odds to "a great chance of success," yet quantifies the NVDA edges that remain (3-bit LUT tensor cores in SM107 vs their absence on MI455X; the **cableless Rubin Oberon rack** as the named reason Helios ramps slowly; the **28.8T NVSwitch** dividing evenly across 72 GPUs vs 432-of-512 lanes wasted on merchant TH6; **~142/159kW vs ~240/257kW** on power) and explains the **HBM4 10.7 Gbps pin-speed gambit** — above JEDEC spec and 40% above AMD's 7.6 Gbps — that caused the earlier Rubin upstream delay (now resolved) and now forces NVDA onto a higher-quality HBM bin. Net: competition hardens, but "the pie is getting massive"; the honest NVDA-negative is organisational, not technical ("Jensen will need to cut bureaucracy… to move faster and defend their lead"). (2) The **07-06 GPU-backstop note was re-read in full** and the modelled economics folded in for the first time — six-year term, 40–60% revenue share above the floor (~18–20% life-of-deal take rate), **cloud service agreements to $77.5B (F1/27) → $175.3B (F1/29)** as an **off-balance-sheet contingent guarantee** (~$5.9B per 100MW), **incremental revenue $1.8B → $13.9B**, and the neocloud IRR calibration (25.4% backstopped vs 40.7% not; DSCR ≥1.3x at 70–80% LTV) — plus the program's own scale-up history ($860M Oct-25 → $3.5B Jan-26). No PT/rating/house/BBG estimate superseded by either. **08-03 (run-inbox, the PRIMARY Fubon call transcript) closes the quarter's Rubin thread on a different note than the relays did. The relays made this a silicon story — volumes cut, KYEC guiding down, HBM4 repriced. The primary transcript makes it a RACK story: the goal (144 GPUs/rack, 576 compute dies) has not moved, but the thing that is unfinished is heat dissipation, cable design and datacenter power density at ~1MW per rack, with Hon Hai still submitting competing two-rack and four-rack proposals and the Rubin Ultra rack design not due until ~December quarter 2027 while Rubin racks start shipping this December quarter. That reframes the standing "is the roadmap slipping?" debate: the silicon roadmap keeps getting reaffirmed (by NVDA, Jensen personally, and five sell-side checks) and yet the system around it is genuinely not designed, which is why both sides of the argument have been able to cite real evidence all quarter. Two housekeeping corrections the primary forces: Q3 Rubin is 200-300K in the transcript vs 300-400K in the relay (hold both), and the $78-80k Rubin ASP is an output of an assumed 75-80% chip GM, not a price — so it must stop being cited as GM evidence. The genuinely NEW positive is on the memory side and it is not NVDA-specific: ODMs (Hon Hai + one ASR-garbled Taiwanese ODM) see NO 2027 memory spec change at all, "including the Google, AWS," and are budgeting purchasing at the full $7-8M/rack (vs ~$3-3.5M for GB200-class) while raising their own capex to fund buy-and-sell of GPUs for 2027 — three independent behavioural tells that the despec narrative is not happening. No PT/rating/house/BBG estimate superseded.

**Barron's narrative arc across the quarter (retroactively filled, 4 issues 05-18→06-22):** reading the four Barron's issues in sequence tells its own story — 05-18 previewed the print bullishly (avg PT $270, bull ladder to $340-390) but explicitly flagged the "AI bubble" search-trend risk and an ~80%-growth bar to clear; 05-20 the print beat that bar; 05-25's post-mortem showed *why* the stock still fell ~3% over two days — hyperscaler growth (+115%) outpacing ACIE (+74%) directly contradicts management's own stated goal ("we should be growing faster than hyperscale capex"), the same tension now logged as a **✗ contested** row in Signal-vs-management above; 06-01's cover story ("Rally in Chips") and 06-22's CEO profile both pivot back to pure valuation-arithmetic bullishness (PEG 0.28x, $217B consensus profit, sub-17x 2027 P/E vs Cerebras' ~280x) — i.e. the sell-side/financial-media narrative cooled on the growth-mix optics in late May before re-anchoring on "it's just cheap" by June. Net read-through for the wiki's PT tracking: this Barron's-sourced range ($270 avg / $290-390 bull ladder / $425 Tigress high) is **additive context, not a house PT change** — no number on this page is superseded.
