  🔴 **THE FINDING: a piece of open-source software (TileRT) removes most of the ultra-low-latency deficit that justified an entire class of competing silicon. On the InferenceX GLM-5.1 benchmark an EIGHT-GPU B200 NODE reaches 340 tok/s/user at 8k/1k — 1.9× the previous best of 181.4, which required a 72-GPU GB300 NVL72 with NVFP4+MTP — and 494.2 tok/s/user at 1k/1k with FP8 (3.6× the best conventional FP8), "and TileRT DOESN'T YET HAVE FP4 SUPPORT." Mechanism: the whole decode graph statically compiled into ONE PERSISTENT KERNEL — "a CUDA graph optimizes the LAUNCHING of kernels; TileRT ABOLISHES THE KERNEL AS THE UNIT OF EXECUTION."**
  ✅ **THE COMMERCIAL NUMBER, which is why it is bullish rather than merely interesting: $13.56 PER MILLION OUTPUT TOKENS at 340 tok/s/user versus $13.4 at 176 tok/s/user for the best conventional FP4 point — 1% more cost for 1.9× the interactivity — and 61% CHEAPER than the fastest conventional FP8 endpoint (~$35/M) at 3.1× the interactivity. Benchmarked explicitly against a lab's premium tier: "much more value than CLAUDE CODE FAST MODE, up to 2.5× the interactivity but 2× the price per token."** **Strategic core: "A GPU POOL IS ONE LIQUID RESOURCE… capacity moving between roles as a SOFTWARE SCHEDULER DECISION… AN ASIC FLEET IS THE OPPOSITE: the ratio is FIXED IN HARDWARE THE DAY THE PURCHASE ORDER IS SIGNED." Production proof: Xiaomi MiMo V2.5 Pro UltraSpeed and Z.ai GLM-5.1 HighSpeed, both on clusters they already owned.**
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE UNRESOLVED TENSION LOGGED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED, because it is specific to NVIDIA and the note does not name it: the TAM this attacks is one NVIDIA NOW SELLS INTO ITSELF. The note refers to "NVIDIA GROQ LPUs" and to "quarter-by-quarter estimates of NVIDIA LPU30, LPU40, [[CEREBRAS]] WSE-3 & WSE-4 shipments." If a B200/GB200 fleet can serve the speed tier at iso-cost, the first casualty inside Nvidia's own book is the LPU line the Groq acquisition bought. NOT adopted as a call — flagged as a question for the LPU roadmap.**
  ⚠️ **Limits recorded: batch size 1 (one in-flight request per decode node in v0.1.5), lower aggregate throughput than a concurrency-12 GB300 point (160.4 vs ~240 tok/s/GPU), a two-model catalog, and pinned driver stacks. And the ceiling is physics: "software can approach the HBM roofline, BUT IT CANNOT RAISE IT."** ✅ **NET-NEW WATCH ITEM WITH A DATE-ABLE OUTCOME: "NVIDIA HAS COMMITTED TO SUBMITTING VERIFIABLE VERA RUBIN NUMBERS TO INFERENCEX" ([[GOOG]] TPUv7 "soon"; [[AMD]] MI455X UALoE72 "this year") — first-party Rubin performance in a public third-party benchmark is checkable.** **Also filed to the memory debate: "memory bandwidth increases 2–3× EACH GENERATION [but] MEMORY LATENCY HAS NOT IMPROVED AT ALL… even as HBM PRICES CONTINUALLY INCREASE."** Cross-filed [[CEREBRAS]], [[AMD]], [[OPENAI]], [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md), [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md). - **2026-07-30 — SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters" (2026-07-29). 1 intra-quarter row (07-29, produto/bull) + 1 Sources entry. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no rating, PT, house estimate or existing NVDA number changed; purely additive.** ⚠️ **Logged as a READ-THROUGH: the note's subject is the modular-datacenter vendor landscape (Vertiv/Flex/nVent/Quanta/Eaton/Schneider et al.), not NVDA.** Routed here because the note documents something the page did not carry at all — **NVDA's reference design has moved from the rack to the building.** What it adds: **DSX**, the reference design for the entire AI factory, dated — Omniverse digital-twin blueprint unveiled at **GTC Washington Oct-2025**, **formalized as the Vera Rubin DSX reference design in March 2026**, since expanded into the full DSX platform, covering compute/networking/storage/hardware-cluster design **plus the facilities side: power, cooling, controls, even civil, structural and architectural design.** The two named products, verbatim: **"Nvidia's DSX Max-Q maximizes token per watt within a fixed power budget, and DSX Flex facilitates the connection [of] the facility to grid services, dynamically adjusting power draw and orchestrating demand with hybrid onsite generation"** — DSX Flex pulls demand-response/BTM orchestration into the reference architecture itself. Deployment workflow via the Omniverse DSX Blueprint (digital twin → simulate layouts, power topologies, thermal behaviour, operational policies → optimize pre-construction → reuse across sites), with **CoreWeave already using DSX Air**. The adoption evidence is the point: Cadence, Dassault Systèmes, Eaton, Jacobs, Nscale, Phaidra, Procore, PTC, Schneider Electric, Siemens, Switch, Trane, Vertiv — **Vertiv's OneCore became the building block of the Vera Rubin DSX reference design as of March 2026** (12.5 MW pods), **Schneider works with NVIDIA on 30+ reference designs** (EcoStruxure Pod, up to 40 racks), **Siemens is among the first suppliers with both IEC and UL variants for Rubin DSX**, **Jacobs is one of only two engineering firms working with the DSX reference architecture (the other Bechtel)**, Hut 8's Beacon Point is designed around DSX, and Foxconn unveiled its Modular Data Center concept at GTC 2026. **Read: moat-widening beyond CUDA — it standardizes the building around NVDA's roadmap, so the switching cost of a competing accelerator now includes the facility. Flip side logged, not smoothed: a reference architecture reaching into civil/structural design also transfers more schedule risk onto NVDA's spec stability.** This also sharpens the PhotonCap caveat logged the same day from the Lumentum meeting ("it rides on one company's architecture choice") — that concentration risk is now documented on the power/cooling/EPC side as well as the optical side. Cross-filed [[VRT]], [[ETN]], [[FLEX]], [[PWR]], [[NVT]], [[CRWV]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power.md](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md), [themes/800v-dc-power.md](themes/800v-dc-power.md).

NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no rating, PT, house estimate or existing NVDA number changed.** ⚠️ **Logged as a READ-THROUGH: the subject company is Lumentum, and the source is a joint independent publication (management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research, technical comments by PhotonCap), not a bank; interview answers are the authors' paraphrase, not verbatim management statements.** **What it adds that this page did not have. (1) A supplier-side explanation of NVDA's CPO motive, from Lumentum management: CPO today is "opportunistic rather than forced. Nvidia deploys it because a faster, more power efficient laser frees up power that can be redirected to the GPUs, which buys system performance. Copper has not run out of headroom yet" — with copper hitting the wall "late 2027," when the first scale-up CPO deployments or MPO connectors arrive, driven by rack-to-rack links beyond ~3m. That CONFIRMS the earlier end of the CPO-timing range and sits against MS's 07-28 markdown of 2028 CPO *scale* and the unresolved Fubon post-Kyber rack decision already on this page; not reconciled. (2) An NVDA-sourced power number quoted by PhotonCap — "Nvidia's own material puts CPO at 3.5x lower power than pluggable transceivers" — with the mechanism stated as a fixed power envelope ("every watt saved on networking is a watt handed to the GPUs") and the conclusion "copper is not forcing this wave; the power budget is." ⚠️ PhotonCap's caveat retained verbatim because it is the systemic risk for the whole optical complex: "the weak spot is not the voluntariness but that it rides on one company's architecture choice." (3) Two Rubin/domain mechanics net-new here: Rubin "relies on bi-directional signaling to reach its bandwidth targets, and bi-directional signaling over copper degrades substantially at 448G per lane," with an unknown time-to-market for 448G copper; and NVDA "has begun moving from 72 GPU domains toward as many as 576 by 2027, and copper is already confined on an NV72 rack" — so once a domain spans racks, "linking must be optical regardless of copper's current efficiency." (4) Second-sourcing posture: "NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent," and "the same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B" — NVDA is not single-sourcing its external light source. Jensen's copper-for-scale-up / photonics-for-scale-out framing and NVDA's stated need for copper capacity both logged.** Cross-filed [LITE.md](LITE.md), [COHR.md](COHR.md), [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md).
- **2026-07-28 (/run-inbox — the Fubon PRIMARY PDF + 2 MS secondaries)** — **3 intra-quarter rows, 1 existing row annotated, Product/Rubin Signal cell extended, 3 Sources links. NO rating, PT or house estimate superseded** (MS Top Pick $288, BofA Buy $350, Barclays OW $275, DB Hold $255, UBS house $280-285, Arete Buy $283 all stand; Fubon's note carries no NVDA rating). **The single most valuable thing this run does is RESOLVE the contradiction this page flagged and deliberately left open earlier today.** The 07-28 `/wiki-ingest` entry below recorded that *"the two Jefferies relays state the 2028 SoIC revision in opposite directions (Wong: to 90k from 70k; Beavington: to 70k from 90k)"* and left it unreconciled. **The primary Fubon PDF settles it verbatim: "Compared to our earlier forecast of 90k capacity in 4Q28, we revise down our capacity expansion forecast to 70k in 4Q28." So 70k is the live mark, 90k the superseded one — Beavington was right, the Wong relay inverted.** The cause is stated too: **[[TSM]] is slowing SoIC expansion to prioritise CoWoS**, which is also why Fubon flags **potential upside to its 220kwpm end-2028 CoWoS number** (end-2027 held at 180kwpm). **A SECOND relay error corrected on the same row: the interposer counts are inverted. Fubon says "12 and 10 interposers per CoWoS wafer for Rubin and Rubin Ultra, respectively" — Rubin 12, Rubin Ultra 10, not the reverse.** The conclusion is unchanged (Rubin Ultra's interposer only marginally larger post-cancellation → chip output 12.4mn 2027 vs 8.2mn 2026), but the numbers on the page were backwards and are now annotated in place rather than deleted. **Net-new #1, and it needs its own guardrail: NVDA's TOTAL capacity allocation is 1.03mn wafers in 2027 vs 629k in 2026 — this is TSMC + SPIL and is BROADER than the TSMC-CoWoS-only line of 945,000 wafers (50.0% of 1.89mn; 586,500 / 50.1% in 2026; 346,800 / 51.4% in 2025). The ~85k gap is SPIL capacity supporting Vera CPU. Do not net the two.** **Net-new #2, a relative-advantage datapoint the page did not have: HBM4 costs NVDA US$31-32/GB (from US$17-18/GB HBM3e in 2026), while "other GPU and custom-ASIC projects… may reach US$35-36 per GB, meaning the HBM cost will double in 2027." HBM inflation is real for NVDA (~+80%) but it buys ~10-15% cheaper than the merchant/ASIC field — a scale edge that partly offsets the ASIC-TCO bear this page carries from JPM/UBS.** On that BOM Fubon models the **Rubin ASP at US$78-80K on a 75-80% chip GM** (confirmed against the Beavington relay, not re-entered). **Net-new #3 — the fullest account on this page of what replaces Kyber, folded into the Product/Rubin Signal cell: Kyber was cancelled/delayed in MARCH/APRIL on mid-plane bandwidth issues; it carried 576 compute dies per rack (144 GPUs × 4 compute dies); checks say NVDA still targets 576 in the replacement, via either (a) four Oberon racks per system on traditional scale-out (144 compute dies each) or (b) a two-rack "Taycan" design at 144 GPUs × 2 compute dies per rack with NPO/CPO for the inter-rack scale-out and cable retained for intra-rack scale-up. Fubon explicitly declines to name winners and losers because neither is finalised — but says "nVidia is still aggressive in the NPO/CPO solution despite a lot of market noise."** Positioning kept in Fubon's own words on both sides: short-term the **NVDA supply chain "could potentially underperform due to Rubin's lower-than-expected volume in 2H26 and uncertain design change in the next generation rack design,"** with Fubon preferring the CPU/SPE/TPU chains — against **"token cost is still a more important factor in driving CSP's capex, and we remain positive about AI market demand."** **Two secondary sources added as separate rows. (1) MS "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC" (Nowak/Byrd/Wood, 07-27) — logged explicitly as a demand-durability read-through and NOT as an NVDA mark (the note has no NVDA rating or PT): MS standardises all three GenAI unit-economics frameworks on 1GW of NVDA GB300 (410,256 chips/GW, US$39bn all-in capex/GW = US$23bn IT on 5yr + US$16bn non-IT on 15yr, ~US$4mn/GB300 rack, 75% utilisation, US$8.50/hr, 2,750 tokens/sec/GPU) and gets ~31% ROIC / ~67% incremental EBIT margin on hyperscaler GPU rental, ~46% / ~75% on a model-enabled API on owned infra, ~25% / ~31% on 3P infra. The buy-side point is that the returns math on the CURRENT generation clears a normal hurdle at every layer — the most direct available answer to the Crisafulli/Chanos/Burry "hyperscalers can't generate FCF" bear. MS's own caveat kept: custom ASICs and prior GPU generations have lower costs, but "chip pricing/hour and token throughput are also lower."** **(2) MS TMT webcast (07-28): Joe Moore's rank order across his coverage is NVDA > AVGO > MU > SNDK — "nothing else that's wildly compelling" — on the argument that the four are "just so much cheaper than every other name that has some speculative potential"; and Lee Simpson names NVIDIA specifically as a user of Cadence's agentic-EDA offering (a design-productivity datapoint, monetisation "really a 2027 story," not folded into any estimate).** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[AVGO]], [[AMD]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[MU]], [[CDNS]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

No rating/PT superseded.** **The substantive change is a SECOND downward revision to Rubin: Jefferies now models ~1mn units this year, cut from 1.5mn**, on the previously-flagged thermal redesign and production push-out — corroborated externally by **KYEC (2449 TT) cutting its FY revenue guide on a 1-2 month Rubin push-out**. Jefferies' read-across is explicit and sector-wide: *“Revising downward Rubin is negative for the entire A.I. sector… direct EU readacross to [[ASML]], ASM, VAT, SUSS, [[IFX]]”* — because **Rubin carries higher dollar content than Blackwell**, a slip costs more per unit than a Blackwell slip would. A **new “Taycan” rack design is being explored as an alternative to Kyber**, layering next-gen rack uncertainty on top of the volume cut. **Offsetting, and important for the out-years: the post-Kyber-cancellation chip-output math actually IMPROVES — Rubin Ultra's interposer is only slightly larger than Rubin (12 per CoWoS wafer vs 10), so total NVDA chip output rises to 12.4mn in 2027E from 8.2mn in 2026E**, and **NVDA's CoWoS allocation share is unchanged at 50%** with additional SPIL support for the Vera CPU. Sherman's HBM4-adjusted BOM now implies a **Rubin GPU price of US$78-80K** at a 75-80% gross margin. ⚠️ **One internal inconsistency left flagged rather than reconciled:** the two Jefferies relays state the 2028 SoIC revision in opposite directions (Wong: to 90k from 70k; Beavington: to 70k from 90k). The direction of travel — SoIC deprioritised in favour of CoWoS, negative for **BESI** as TSMC's hybrid-bonder supplier — is consistent across both; the endpoint is not. **On capital and sentiment: NVDA is investing multiple billions in Ilya Sutskever's Safe Superintelligence** (@zerohedge relays **$5bn**; ⚠️ wire figure, not company-confirmed), and the market treated it as fresh evidence for the **circular-financing** bear rather than as a strategic positive. Cramer's line is the cleanest statement of the current regime: *“if there were no financing involved Nvidia's stock would be soaring… all that matters is the financing, BECAUSE of memories of 2000.”* Also logged: **Taiwan prosecutors detained an NVIDIA employee in a chip-smuggling case**; **Moonshot seeking more Blackwell for its next model**. MS's desk records the buy-side reaction verbatim — **"big numbers but feeds into 'circularity' concerns / NVDA becoming the 'bank of AI'"** — and Rothschild calls it **"the mother of all circular/vendor financing deals."** **(2) The genuinely valuable item is The Information's audit of exactly those headlines, and it is filed as a direct counterweight rather than netted:** Peers argues **neither deal was news and neither is fleshed out** — **NVDA and SK have signed only letters of intent**, and the precedent is NVDA's own (**an OpenAI LOI last September for a "landmark strategic partnership" was abandoned a few months later**); the Friday SK release was **mostly a reprise of two early-June announcements** with the **only real change being the Korean AI cloud doubling 1 GW → 2 GW**; NVDA **will not disclose who is funding what** in the $500B; and the WSJ OpenAI story **had already been reported by The Information in early June**. **Practical implication for how this page reads deal headlines going forward: LOI ≠ backlog, and re-announcement inflation is now a documented pattern at this company.** **(3) Net-new product evidence, and the first from the customer side: The Information (Phoebe Liu) has cloud executives testing early Vera Rubin racks expecting them "much easier to install" than Grace Blackwell — "mechanically and thermally, it is now a third generation product"** (same 72 GPU / 36 CPU shape), **≥2x the GB rack price for a claimed 10x tokens/s/W**. - **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch). 6 intra-quarter rows. No rating, PT or estimate superseded** (MS Top Pick $288, BofA $320/$350, Barclays OW $275, Cantor all stand). **Two items dominate and they point in opposite directions, which is the honest state of the name.** **(1) THE BACKSTOP GOES TO $250BN — reported, not announced. WSJ says Nvidia is in talks to provide a $250bn backstop for [[OPENAI]] to help it lease a 10GW Ohio data-center project from SoftBank, with the project costing ~$500bn including the chips.** This is the **exact mechanism already documented on this page from SemiAnalysis's "AI Project Trinity" (07-06), scaled to one counterparty and one site, at roughly an order of magnitude above anything previously disclosed** — NVDA guarantees the lease, SoftBank owns the asset, OpenAI takes the capacity, NVDA books the chips. **Status is talks per WSJ; not confirmed by NVDA, OpenAI or SoftBank, and NOT carried here as a commitment.** JPM's framing is the one worth keeping, because it inverts the usual read: this is a *test* of the tape, not a catalyst — "the sum of money in discussion is gargantuan… **provides yet another test of whether 'good news' around capex can lift semi stocks. If another $250bn guarantee isn't enough, then well… you really have to wonder what is**" — with the SOX already **-4.3% Friday and -19% off the mid-June high**. It will, in JPM's words, "invariably raise circular financing debates again." **(2) THE MEMORY BILL IS NOW VISIBLY CUTTING NVDA'S OWN SPECS, and this is the clearest margin-transfer mechanism yet logged on this page.** Jefferies' O'Mara, citing WCCFTECH: after the reported **RTX 50 ramp delay on memory costs** and the **SOCAMM spec cut**, NVDA is **now also cutting the memory spec on NVL72** — because **"memory is the majority of the BOM on VR200."** The dispersion in sell-side inputs is the striking part: rack-cost models are running on **HBM $/GB assumptions from ~$20 (Jefferies' own Sherman, which O'Mara says is too low) to $53/GB in 2027 — a ~2.7x spread** — with one broker at **~$9.1m per rack**. O'Mara's conclusion: "Nvidia has a memory problem in its BOM and earnings structure… trying to address this by reducing the specs across multiple products." Trade-press + desk opinion, not NVDA disclosure — but it is the first *observable* evidence that the memory upcycle this book is long elsewhere ([[MU]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SNDK]]) is being paid for partly out of NVDA's bill of materials. **(3) A clean policy split now sits between the compute layer and the model layer: NVDA published an open letter urging Washington NOT to impose sweeping restrictions on open-weight models (with MSFT co-leading, post-Kimi K3), while [[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] are reported to be lobbying FOR restrictions** ("increasingly rattled by China's advances"; the administration leaning toward per-model national-security review rather than a blanket ban — NYT). Consistent with UBS's read that at the GPU layer open-model traction reallocates rather than reduces demand and is **"bullish Nvidia."** **(4) Two expert calls with hard engineering numbers: next-gen GPUs reaching up to 2,400W TDP with D2C liquid cooling the current standard and advanced alternatives (two-phase immersion, TEC, new TIMs) "two to three years away" from commercial deployment; and the cost governor that cooling "should not exceed 9-10% of total data center operating or CapEx costs."** Separately, an MSFT data-center program manager puts **solid-state transformers as the eventual 800V DC standard but "not… widespread adoption before 2030-2032"** — ⚠️ **materially later than the vendor timeline on [[GEV]]'s page** (5MW indoor SST shipping to a hyperscaler this year, 6MW outdoor over 18 months, revenue "'27 and beyond"). Logged as a vendor-vs-buyer gap, not resolved. **(5) New non-tech verticals: BMY deploying Vera Rubin racks and a 5-year LLY/NVDA agreement** — pharma is new to this page — plus real-world token evidence from **AT&T at ~1tn+ tokens/month / ~16.4tn annualized, >100 GenAI models in production (~40% on AMD hardware)**. Also: **Feynman GPU + Rosa CPU targeting TSMC A16 around 2028** (Commercial Times, trade press). BofA's **NVDA buy-side bogey poll is open into the 8/26 AMC print**. Cross-filed [[OPENAI]], [[MU]], [[TSM]], [[AMD]], [[GEV]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/800v-dc-power](themes/800v-dc-power.md).
